## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

Runtime history acknowledgment is independent of successful compiler snapshots.
First check delivers history once even on refusal; upgrade baseline checks cannot
consume it, and install-first cannot overwrite older compiler facts. Refuse an
implicit downgrade before compilation/install, with an explicit --to remedy.
Human upgrade runs human check; --json emits one object and puts progress/history
on stderr. LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and its packaged-proof child record this.

Dormant row populations cover their own retained cells, bytes and assets; only
active rows contribute standalone column cells/samples. The archived-row example
is one row plus two cells. Audit keeps actual restoration counters and distinguishes
a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

@@ -129,6 +129,28 @@ A diagnostic names the condition that failed.
 
 Spellings are illustrative. None of the blocks below is a compiling example.
 
+### 3.0 Aggregate target identity and migration
+
+Every ordinary maintained aggregate row has one internal identity function:
+64 lowercase hexadecimal characters from SHA-256 over the domain
+`snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1` and the typed canonical group-key vector.
+The checker therefore admits the largest legal group key against a fixed-size
+identity, and synthetic check rows, incremental writes, point reads and
+adoption rebuilds all call the same function. The group key remains the public
+address; programs do not read or supply this internal id.
+
+The store metadata marker `maintained_id_version = sha256-v1` binds persisted
+aggregate targets to that function. Opening or adopting a store without the
+marker — including a 0.4.28 store whose ids were the unbounded canonical JSON
+of the group key — atomically clears and rebuilds every ordinary aggregate
+target from its source rows, writes the marker, and publishes a new semantic
+generation. A failure rolls back old rows, rebuilt rows, marker and generation
+together. Adoption advice counts that rebuild even when schema/program bytes
+are unchanged. Schemas with ordinary maintains require device capability
+`maintained-id-sha256-v1`; an older device is refused at the handshake before
+it can compute a legacy id and silently read a digest-addressed aggregate as
+zero.
+
 ### 3.1 F1: maintained columns
 
 F1 is LLP 3000.007.001, unchanged. Its value here is ranking. With `likeCount`

 `sweep_expired(now)` consults only `expire at`; it never treats unopened rows as
-expired. When the expiry is due, deletion removes the row and pending-opening
-entry in one transaction. Deleting or expiring a never-opened row creates no
-device event, because no device could hold it. Deleting an opened row keeps the
+expired. When the expiry is due, retirement removes the root and pending-opening
+entry and applies its declared `on delete cascade`, `set null` and `restrict`
+actions in one transaction (LLP 3000.039 §3). A live restrict child or physical
+read-through child may retain the root physically, but the expired root remains
+absent from all logical reads. Cleanup advances past such retained roots and
+preserves its bounded scan position across calls; cascade/set-null writes and
+the root removal either commit together or roll back together. The same action
+path governs a row born expired at commit and an expired predecessor retired
+for a same-id insertion, so neither inline cleanup path can create an orphan.
+
+Expiring a never-opened root creates no root deletion event, because no device
+could hold it. Its actions can still change opened children, and those ordinary
+row changes carry their partition events. Deleting an opened row keeps the
 ordinary revocation and retention semantics.
 Expiry cleanup pins the authoritative floor but commits its physical removals
 before attempting the next opening. A refused opening therefore cannot wedge

Every walk streams pages through the reference index and keeps identities only; a restrict probe streams one row per seek past the closure, the root included, to its first outside witness; set null rereads each row before clearing it; the reply's event count is decided by the static action envelope; the reservation prices the pages and charges output. Field-owned references carry no action, stated. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: folds the second delta round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. §5.1 is restated meter by meter, as the code computes it, with the per-probe witness, two seeks for every expiring discovery and identity-only retention. The reference index seeks a table's effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`). Every removal, the root included, uses the row's current image. Row-keyed view member tables are refused as cascade and set-null targets. The §4.4 guard is noted as incompatible with `set null`. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: folds the delta round, GPT and Muse both NOT READY. The reservation is defined from schema-fixed closure bounds: closure identities, closure-skipping probes, read-through seeks and a physical allowance, with heap as a maximum. The published touched set includes every derived destination, and the reply omits every row the actions changed. Cascade and set null are refused into tables that feed a maintained sum, have any field-owned column, or belong to a timeline in any role. `set null` is refused where any rule hops through the column. A device needs the referencing group acquired, not only readable. The removal order follows cascade and `read through` edges, whose union must be acyclic. `check` names the read prerequisite where a deleter may not be a reader (`N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ`). Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: folds one blind round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. `on delete keep` is added for deliberate history. A delete evaluates its whole cascade closure before it refuses, so a diamond or a sibling reference never refuses spuriously, and the reported reference is chosen in schema order, never by hidden iteration order. `set null` may clear only a column that carries no authority anywhere in the schema. Every published trace of a delete with actions — touched tables, change footprints, event counts, the physical authorization allowance — is fixed by the schema. The reservation reuses the exported source-write price, so a device consumes the same price the server computes. A null-exempt cap never proves a population bound. Cascade and set null into append-only, field-owned or unpriced children are refused. The postcondition is scoped to the rows present when the delete runs. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (draft)
+**Revised:** 2026-10-14 (Wave 12 P1: expiry retirement applies reference actions transactionally and bounded sweeps progress past retained roots); 2026-10-12 (Integration 3: retained event allowance counts every union arm); 2026-10-12 (revision 8: revision-7 GPT findings — export event prices/envelopes before private derivations are projected away, price receiver transition steps, and accept only stable name/literal proof keys; Muse cascade-through paired regression added); 2026-10-12 (revision 7: revision-6 GPT findings — schema-fixed pending-event heap retained for the request, audience-transition tables included in the publication envelope, stable bindings in every child-inserting status-proof program; Muse lifetime-seek term fixed); 2026-10-12 (revision 6: fresh revision-5 GPT findings — action hooks run within the prepaid physical scope, lifetime-hook heap overlaps are reserved, derived private-field delivery targets are refused, and status aliases require stable bindings); 2026-10-12 (revision 5: round-5 GPT fixes — gather set-null groups before writing, bypass child read rules in constraints, sum concurrent write heap reservations, queue retained device history, and prove count guards at each delete site; reconciles development reset and the retired orphan note); 2026-10-12 (renumbered from 3000.038, which file assets took when 0.4.23 merged; review files renamed to match); 2026-10-12 (revision 4: folds the third delta round, a review of revision 3 with its implementation: GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. Every walk streams pages through the reference index and keeps identities only; a restrict probe streams one row per seek past the closure, the root included, to its first outside witness; set null rereads each row before clearing it; the reply's event count is decided by the static action envelope; the reservation prices the pages and charges output. Field-owned references carry no action, stated. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: folds the second delta round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. §5.1 is restated meter by meter, as the code computes it, with the per-probe witness, two seeks for every expiring discovery and identity-only retention. The reference index seeks a table's effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`). Every removal, the root included, uses the row's current image. Row-keyed view member tables are refused as cascade and set-null targets. The §4.4 guard is noted as incompatible with `set null`. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: folds the delta round, GPT and Muse both NOT READY. The reservation is defined from schema-fixed closure bounds: closure identities, closure-skipping probes, read-through seeks and a physical allowance, with heap as a maximum. The published touched set includes every derived destination, and the reply omits every row the actions changed. Cascade and set null are refused into tables that feed a maintained sum, have any field-owned column, or belong to a timeline in any role. `set null` is refused where any rule hops through the column. A device needs the referencing group acquired, not only readable. The removal order follows cascade and `read through` edges, whose union must be acyclic. `check` names the read prerequisite where a deleter may not be a reader (`N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ`). Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: folds one blind round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. `on delete keep` is added for deliberate history. A delete evaluates its whole cascade closure before it refuses, so a diamond or a sibling reference never refuses spuriously, and the reported reference is chosen in schema order, never by hidden iteration order. `set null` may clear only a column that carries no authority anywhere in the schema. Every published trace of a delete with actions — touched tables, change footprints, event counts, the physical authorization allowance — is fixed by the schema. The reservation reuses the exported source-write price, so a device consumes the same price the server computes. A null-exempt cap never proves a population bound. Cascade and set null into append-only, field-owned or unpriced children are refused. The postcondition is scoped to the rows present when the delete runs. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (draft)
 **Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (§7, 2026-09-13 "reference IDs have no implicit foreign-key check": this RFC amends its delete half), [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md) (§15.8, the open question this answers; §16.2, the zero-count guard), [LLP 3000.014](./3000.014-a-child-reads-through-its-parent.rfc.md) (§3, a child lives within its parent; §5, predicted parent deletes; §6, `E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN`), [LLP 3000.015](./3000.015-shared-baseline-for-snapback5.spec.md) (§2, one authorization law: work whose shape depends on unreadable rows is charged from a fixed reservation), [LLP 3000.022](./3000.022-delivery-the-sdk-reports.rfc.md) (§14.3, one exported source-write price), [LLP 3000.023](./3000.023-answers-stay-on-screen.rfc.md) (§12, change evidence), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) (§20.5, held-group proofs for device writes), [LLP 3000.035](./3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md) (§5.2, the Lean model)
 
 ## Summary
@@ -262,10 +262,25 @@ Every step is inside the mutation's transaction: a refusal anywhere (a
 `restrict`, a read-through child, the meter) aborts the whole mutation, and
 nothing commits.
 
-**Expiry is not a delete.** A parent whose `expire at` passes is absent from
-that moment, and the sweep removes it physically; neither fires an action.
-A child of an expiring parent outlives it unless the child declares the same
-lifetime (`read through`, or its own `expire at`). The writes card says so.
+**Expiry is a referential-action trigger.** A parent whose `expire at` passes
+is logically absent at that instant, independent of whether physical cleanup
+can retire it. Every path that physically retires an expired image — scheduled
+sweep, an already-expired birth at commit, and same-id replacement of an
+expired predecessor — applies steps 3–6 above in the same transaction. It
+bypasses only the expired root's authored read/delete authorization; it does
+not bypass `cascade`, `set null`, `restrict`, read-through retention,
+constraints, maintains or partition events on affected rows.
+
+A live `restrict` child keeps the expired root physically stored and the sweep
+continues to later due keys. The retained root remains logically unreadable.
+Short sweeps preserve a per-table scan continuation, so repeated bounded calls
+cannot restart forever at the same restricted or read-through-retained roots;
+after reaching the end they wrap for roots whose blockers were removed.
+Expired children are not live restrict witnesses, as in step 4. A read-through
+child remains the stricter physical exception because its stored image depends
+on its parent. Any error after an earlier cascade or set-null write rolls back
+the whole retirement transaction. A restricted root alone is an expected
+deferred cleanup, not an error for the sweep as a whole.
 
 ## 4. Authorization and disclosure

-- Actions on expiry.
 - A report of existing dangling references at adoption (§8).
 - `set null` on a column that carries authority, with a declared transfer
   rule.

+    pub(super) fn retire_expired_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
+        if self.physical_children(table_name, old.field("id")?)? { return Ok(false); }
+        let current = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {

+    }

-/// The id of a maintained row is its group's canonical form, as Snapback 2
-/// spelled it: an aggregate's identity is its key, and the conformance
-/// corpus holds it to this spelling. Programs read aggregates by group,
-/// never by id.
+/// Activation marker for the maintained-row address function. A missing or
+/// older marker rebuilds every aggregate target before the generation opens.
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_META: &str = "maintained_id_version";
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION: &str = "sha256-v1";
+
+/// The id of a maintained row is a fixed-size digest of its typed group key.
+/// Programs address aggregates by their group index, never by this internal
+/// id. Domain separation makes future encodings explicit migrations.
+// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#30-aggregate-target-identity-and-migration — every aggregate writer, synthetic check row and adoption rebuild shares this bounded identity.
 pub fn maintained_id(key: &[Value]) -> String {
-    crate::value::canonical_json(&key)
+    crate::value::digest(&("snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1", key))
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn maintained_identity_is_fixed_size_and_domain_separated() {
+    let short = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".into())]);
+    let long = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".repeat(100_000))]);
+    assert_eq!(short.len(), 64);
+    assert_eq!(long.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(short, long);
+    assert_ne!(short, crate::value::digest(&[Value::String("x".into())]));
 }

+#[test]
+fn missing_identity_marker_rebuilds_a_principal_reference_and_max_text_group() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table parents:\n  public\ntable entries:\n  owner: principal\n  parent: parents\n  token: text <=64\n  amount: int\n  public\nview totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    let owner = format!("user:{}", "o".repeat(59));
+    let parent = "p".repeat(64);
+    let token = "t".repeat(64);
+    assert_eq!((owner.len(), parent.len(), token.len()), (64, 64, 64));
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))])),
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("owner", Value::Id(owner.clone())),
+            ("parent", Value::Id(parent.clone())), ("token", Value::String(token.clone())), ("amount", Value::Int(7))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(owner), Value::Id(parent), Value::String(token)]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+    let after = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn identity_redeployment_rebuilds_the_ungrouped_empty_tuple() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  public\nview total: count entries\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    e.seed_at([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into()))]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    let after = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+}

+#[test]
+fn aggregate_identity_capability_upgrades_legacy_wire_and_fences_old_devices() {
+    let schema = compile("table entries:\n  group: text <=64\n  public\nview totals: count entries by .group\n  public\n");
+    assert!(schema.required_capabilities().contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"));
+
+    // A pre-migration stored schema may already have a carrier for unrelated
+    // capabilities. Decode it once, then stamp the digest identity capability
+    // on every newly published schema/projection.
+    let mut legacy = serde_json::to_value(&schema).unwrap();
+    for table in legacy["tables"].as_object_mut().unwrap().values_mut() {
+        for constraint in table["constraints"].as_array_mut().unwrap() {
+            if let Some(names) = constraint.get_mut("Capabilities").and_then(|value| value.as_array_mut()) {
+                names.retain(|value| value != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    let upgraded: Schema = serde_json::from_value(legacy).unwrap();
+    let encoded = serde_json::to_string(&upgraded).unwrap();
+    assert!(encoded.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"));
+
+    let required = snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&upgraded.public_projection());
+    let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+    old.version = "0.4.28".into();
+    old.capabilities.retain(|capability| capability != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+    let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION");
+    assert!(refusal.message.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"), "{refusal:?}");
+}
+

 fn run<H: Host>(host: &mut H) {
+    // Server expiry is a physical retirement with the schema's declared
+    // referential effects. Devices receive that one committed closure just as
+    // they receive an authored parent delete.
+    let expiring = r#"
+table parents:
+  until: time
+  expire at .until
+  public 'parents'
+  sync public
+table cascadeChildren:
+  parent: parents on delete cascade
+  public 'cascadeChildren'
+  sync public
+table nullableChildren:
+  parent: parents ? on delete set null
+  public 'nullableChildren'
+  sync public
+"#;
+    let (mut e, b) = compile(expiring);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("p")), ("until", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeChildren".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c")), ("parent", id("p"))])),
+        ("nullableChildren".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("n")), ("parent", id("p"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let mut d = host.open("expiry-actions", Some(b)).unwrap();
+    acquire(&mut e, &mut d, "alice", 0);
+    let from = d.state().unwrap().0;
+    assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 100).unwrap(), 1);
+    d.apply(&stream_page(&mut e, "alice", from), false).unwrap();
+    assert!(d.store().raw_rows("parents").is_empty());
+    assert!(d.store().raw_rows("cascadeChildren").is_empty());
+    let nullable = d.store().raw_rows("nullableChildren");
+    assert_eq!(nullable.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(nullable[0].field("parent").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Null);
+    host.close("expiry-actions", d);
+
     // Every referencing group is public and held: the cascade predicts, deleting the held tasks.
     let (mut e, b) = compile(PUBLIC);
     assert_eq!(write(&mut e, "setup", "alice", &[]), "ok");
@@ -173,6 +209,55 @@ mutation drop(id: parents):
     host.close("retained-cascade", d);
 }
 
+#[test]
+fn authoritative_memory_sweeps_resume_and_wrap_past_restricted_roots() {
+    let source = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+table cascadeKids:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'cascade kids'
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+table blockers:
+  root: roots
+  public 'blockers'
+"#;
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let mut store = snapback4_device::memory::MemoryStore::new();
+    store.activate_server_at(bound.schema, 0).unwrap();
+    store.seed_server_at([
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("a-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("b-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c-blocked")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("z-free")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("cz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("nz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("ba")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("bb")), ("root", id("b-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("bc")), ("root", id("c-blocked"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let wrapped_removed: u64 = (0..4).map(|_| store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap()).sum();
+    assert_eq!(wrapped_removed, 1, "continuation reaches the free root within one bounded turn per due root");
+    assert!(store.raw_rows("cascadeKids").is_empty());
+    assert_eq!(store.raw_rows("nullableKids")[0].field("root").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Null);
+
+    // The cursor is after z. A lower new key is found after the scan wraps;
+    // each restricted root still consumes one bounded visit.
+    store.seed_server_at([("roots".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", id("aa-free")), ("expiresAt", snapback4_core::Value::Int(10)),
+    ]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let wrapped_removed: u64 = (0..4).map(|_| store.sweep_server_at(10, 1).unwrap()).sum();
+    assert_eq!(wrapped_removed, 1, "a wrapped scan reaches the new lower key within one bounded turn per due root");
+    assert!(!store.raw_rows("roots").iter().any(|row| row.field("id").unwrap() == &id("aa-free")));
+}
+
 #[test] fn reference_actions_memory() { run(&mut Memory::new()); }
 #[test] fn reference_actions_sqlite() { run(&mut Sqlite::new("reference-actions")); }
 
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..05a91f6ddf
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-sim/tests/expiry_reference_actions.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,140 @@
+//! Expiry is a physical delete trigger for referential actions (LLP 3000.039
+//! §3). These cases cover scheduled cleanup, commit-end retirement and
+//! same-id replacement of an expired row against the real engine.
+use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, value::object};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+
+fn id(value: &str) -> Value { Value::Id(value.into()) }
+
+fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap()
+}
+
+const SWEEP: &str = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+  sync public
+table cascadeKids:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'cascade kids'
+  sync public
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 4 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+  sync public
+table blockers:
+  root: roots
+  public 'blockers'
+  sync public
+"#;
+
+#[test]
+fn sweep_applies_every_action_and_advances_past_a_restricted_root() {
+    let mut e = engine(SWEEP);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("a-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("b-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("c-blocked")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("z-free")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("ca")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("cz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), object([("id", id("nz")), ("root", id("z-free"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("ba")), ("root", id("a-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("bb")), ("root", id("b-blocked"))])),
+        ("blockers".into(), object([("id", id("bc")), ("root", id("c-blocked"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+
+    for expected in [0, 0, 0, 1] {
+        assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 1).unwrap(), expected, "a one-row sweep must advance its physical scan position");
+    }
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().iter().map(|r| r.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec!["a-blocked", "b-blocked", "c-blocked"]);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().iter().map(|r| r.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), vec!["ca"]);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+
+    for blocker in ["ba", "bb", "bc"] { e.seed_delete("blockers", blocker).unwrap(); }
+    assert_eq!(e.sweep_expired(10, 10).unwrap(), 3);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty());
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn a_late_cascade_refusal_rolls_back_earlier_set_null_writes() {
+    let source = r#"
+table roots:
+  expiresAt: time ?
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  public 'roots'
+table nullableKids:
+  root: roots ? on delete set null
+  cap 1 by root
+  public 'nullable kids'
+table children:
+  root: roots on delete cascade
+  cap 1 by root
+  public 'children'
+table grandchildren:
+  child: children
+  read through .child
+"#;
+    let mut e = engine(source);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("p")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(10))])),
+        ("nullableKids".into(), object([("id", id("n")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("children".into(), object([("id", id("c")), ("root", id("p"))])),
+        ("grandchildren".into(), object([("id", id("g")), ("child", id("c"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+
+    let error = e.sweep_expired(10, 10).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(error.code(), "E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN", "{error:?}");
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &id("p"), "the prior set-null write rolled back");
+    for table in ["roots", "children", "grandchildren"] {
+        assert_eq!(e.store().rows(table).unwrap().len(), 1, "{table}");
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn commit_retirement_and_expired_same_id_replacement_apply_actions() {
+    let source = format!(r#"{SWEEP}
+mutation bornExpired():
+  insert roots {{id: 'born', expiresAt: now}}
+  insert cascadeKids {{id: 'born-c', root: 'born'}}
+  insert nullableKids {{id: 'born-n', root: 'born'}}
+  return null
+mutation bornRestricted():
+  insert roots {{id: 'blocked', expiresAt: now}}
+  insert blockers {{id: 'blocked-b', root: 'blocked'}}
+  return null
+mutation replaceExpired():
+  insert roots {{id: 'old', expiresAt: null}}
+  return null
+"#);
+    let mut e = engine(&source);
+    let sent = e.mutate("bornExpired", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(sent.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{:?}", sent.state);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty());
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("nullableKids").unwrap()[0].field("root").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+
+    let refused = e.mutate("bornRestricted", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(refused.state, WriteState::Failed { ref why, .. } if why.code == "E_REFERENCED"), "{:?}", refused.state);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().is_empty(), "the refused birth and its blocker roll back together");
+    assert!(e.store().rows("blockers").unwrap().is_empty());
+
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("roots".into(), object([("id", id("old")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("cascadeKids".into(), object([("id", id("old-c")), ("root", id("old"))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let replaced = e.mutate("replaceExpired", None, Context::new("dev:alice", BTreeMap::new()).at(100)).unwrap();
+    assert!(matches!(replaced.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{:?}", replaced.state);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap().len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("roots").unwrap()[0].field("expiresAt").unwrap(), &Value::Null);
+    assert!(e.store().rows("cascadeKids").unwrap().is_empty(), "same-id cleanup cannot orphan the expired predecessor's child");
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..c6754a981f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/aggregate_identity_upgrade.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,175 @@
+//! A store written by the frozen 0.4.28 binary adopts digest-addressed
+//! aggregate rows before the current engine serves it.
+use rusqlite::Connection;
+use std::{
+    fs,
+    path::{Path, PathBuf},
+    process::Command,
+};
+
+const SOURCE: &str = r#"use personas alice
+table entries:
+  group: text <=256
+  amount: int
+  insert <- allow
+  public
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .group
+  public
+mutation add(group: text <=256, amount: int):
+  insert entries {group, amount}
+query total(group: text <=256):
+  return totals[group]
+"#;
+
+struct Temp(PathBuf);
+impl Temp {
+    fn new() -> Self {
+        let path = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
+            "snapback4-maintained-id-{}-{}",
+            std::process::id(),
+            std::time::SystemTime::now()
+                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
+                .unwrap()
+                .as_nanos()
+        ));
+        fs::create_dir_all(path.join("snapback")).unwrap();
+        fs::write(path.join("snapback/schema.q"), SOURCE).unwrap();
+        Self(path)
+    }
+}
+impl Drop for Temp {
+    fn drop(&mut self) {
+        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
+    }
+}
+
+fn aggregate_id(store: &Path) -> String {
+    Connection::open(store)
+        .unwrap()
+        .query_row(
+            "SELECT r.id FROM r JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=r.t WHERE c.name='totals'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[ignore = "requires frozen 0.4.28 binary via SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN"]
+fn a_0_4_28_store_rebuilds_legacy_aggregate_ids_and_fences_old_devices() {
+    let baseline = PathBuf::from(
+        std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN")
+            .expect("set SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN to the frozen 0.4.28 snapback4 binary"),
+    );
+    assert!(
+        baseline.is_file(),
+        "SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN is not a file: {}",
+        baseline.display()
+    );
+    let version = Command::new(&baseline).arg("--version").output().unwrap();
+    assert!(version.status.success());
+    assert!(
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&version.stdout).contains("0.4.28"),
+        "unexpected baseline: {:?}",
+        version
+    );
+
+    let dir = Temp::new();
+    let group = "x".repeat(200);
+    let args = serde_json::json!({"group": group, "amount": 7}).to_string();
+    let written = Command::new(&baseline)
+        .current_dir(&dir.0)
+        .args(["run", "add", &args, "--as", "alice", "--now", "1", "--json"])
+        .output()
+        .unwrap();
+    assert!(
+        written.status.success(),
+        "{}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&written.stderr)
+    );
+    let store = dir.0.join(".snapback4/store.sqlite");
+    let legacy = aggregate_id(&store);
+    assert!(
+        legacy.len() > snapback4_core::rows::MAX_ID_BYTES,
+        "the frozen binary must have written the oversized legacy address"
+    );
+    let before: u64 = Connection::open(&store)
+        .unwrap()
+        .query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get::<_, String>(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+        .parse()
+        .unwrap();
+
+    let opened = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4"))
+        .current_dir(&dir.0)
+        .args([
+            "run",
+            "total",
+            &serde_json::json!({"group": group}).to_string(),
+            "--as",
+            "alice",
+            "--now",
+            "2",
+            "--json",
+        ])
+        .output()
+        .unwrap();
+    assert!(
+        opened.status.success(),
+        "stdout={} stderr={}",
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&opened.stdout),
+        String::from_utf8_lossy(&opened.stderr)
+    );
+    let answer: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_slice(&opened.stdout).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(answer["data"]["value"], 7, "{answer}");
+
+    let digest = aggregate_id(&store);
+    assert_eq!(digest.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(digest, legacy);
+    let conn = Connection::open(&store).unwrap();
+    let (after, marker): (u64, String) = (
+        conn.query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation'",
+            [],
+            |row| row.get::<_, String>(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap()
+        .parse()
+        .unwrap(),
+        conn.query_row(
+            "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key=?1",
+            [snapback4_core::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META],
+            |row| row.get(0),
+        )
+        .unwrap(),
+    );
+    assert!(
+        after > before,
+        "identity adoption must publish a new device generation"
+    );
+    assert_eq!(marker, snapback4_core::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION);
+
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), SOURCE.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    let required =
+        snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&bound.schema.public_projection());
+    assert!(
+        required
+            .capabilities
+            .contains(&"maintained-id-sha256-v1".to_owned())
+    );
+    let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+    old.version = "0.4.28".into();
+    old.capabilities
+        .retain(|capability| capability != "maintained-id-sha256-v1");
+    let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION");
+    assert!(
+        refusal.message.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1"),
+        "{refusal:?}"
+    );
+}

index 2ff219222e..5f5410bd52 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema.rs
@@ -1740,12 +1740,27 @@ pub fn maintained_key(source: &Value, by: &[String]) -> Vec<Value> {
     by.iter().map(|field| source.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)).collect()
 }
 
-/// The id of a maintained row is its group's canonical form, as Snapback 2
-/// spelled it: an aggregate's identity is its key, and the conformance
-/// corpus holds it to this spelling. Programs read aggregates by group,
-/// never by id.
+/// Activation marker for the maintained-row address function. A missing or
+/// older marker rebuilds every aggregate target before the generation opens.
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_META: &str = "maintained_id_version";
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION: &str = "sha256-v1";
+
+/// The id of a maintained row is a fixed-size digest of its typed group key.
+/// Programs address aggregates by their group index, never by this internal
+/// id. Domain separation makes future encodings explicit migrations.
+// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#30-aggregate-target-identity-and-migration — every aggregate writer, synthetic check row and adoption rebuild shares this bounded identity.
 pub fn maintained_id(key: &[Value]) -> String {
-    crate::value::canonical_json(&key)
+    crate::value::digest(&("snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1", key))
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn maintained_identity_is_fixed_size_and_domain_separated() {
+    let short = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".into())]);
+    let long = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".repeat(100_000))]);
+    assert_eq!(short.len(), 64);
+    assert_eq!(long.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(short, long);
+    assert_ne!(short, crate::value::digest(&[Value::String("x".into())]));
 }
 
 /// Whether a value is (or contains) the reserved system principal.

@@ -70,6 +70,9 @@ const CAPABILITIES: &[(&str, fn(&Schema) -> bool)] = &[
     // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — a decoder that orders only int/time in insert rules refuses a money or decimal order by name.

   566	    pub fn adopt_at(&mut self, mut schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   567	        columns::validate_before_prepare(&schema)?;
   568	        schema.check_row_view_audiences()?;
   569	        schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
   570	        schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
   571	        let issues = crate::schema::validate(&schema);
   572	        if let Some(issue) = issues.first() {
   573	            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}: {}", issue.path, issue.message)));
   574	        }
   575	        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
   576	        drop(self.ephemeral.lock()?);
   577	        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(&mut self.store, &schema, &programs, None, now, true, None, None)?;
   578	        self.timeline_schedule = timeline::Schedule::default();
   579	        self.schema = schema;
   580	        self.programs = programs;
   581	        self.generation = generation;
   582	        self.expiry_after.clear();
   583	        self.ephemeral.rebind_committed(metadata.store_id, generation, &self.schema);
   584	        Ok(generation)
   585	    }

   588	    /// Delete the rows past their `expire` column. They were absent to
   589	    /// every reader from the moment they expired; the sweep makes that
   590	    /// physical, so the log carries the deletion to each device holding
   591	    /// them. At most `limit` rows go in one commit; the next pass continues.
   592	    pub fn sweep_expired(&mut self, now: i64, limit: usize) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   593	        self.sweep_expired_for(now, limit, std::time::Duration::MAX)
   594	    }

   596	    /// Stop between row removals; a short sweep can still leave due rows.
   597	    pub fn sweep_expired_for(&mut self, now: i64, limit: usize, budget: std::time::Duration) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   598	        // Expiry cleanup is recovery work. Pin the same authoritative clock,
   599	        // but commit physical cleanup before attempting a due opening so a
   600	        // refused publication can never wedge the rows whose upper lifetime
   601	        // has already ended.
   602	        let now = self.pin_time_audience(now)?;
   603	        let started = std::time::Instant::now();
   604	        let expiring: Vec<(String, String)> = self.schema.through_order().into_iter().rev().filter(|name| self.schema.tables[name].ephemeral.is_none()).filter_map(|name| {
   605	                self.schema.tables[&name].expiry_index().map(|(index, _)| (name.clone(), index.to_owned()))
   606	            }).collect();
   607	        if expiring.is_empty() {
   608	            return Ok(0);
   609	        }
   610	        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
   611	        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
   612	        let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
   613	        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort), seq);
   614	        let mut removed = 0_u64;
   615	        let mut visited = 0_usize;
   616	        let mut expiry_after = self.expiry_after.clone();
   617	        let due = crate::store::Bounds { gte: Some(Value::Int(i64::MIN)), lte: Some(Value::Int(now)), ..Default::default() };
   618	        let expiring = through_activation::resume_expiry_tables(
   619	            expiring.into_iter().map(|(name, _)| name).collect(), &expiry_after,
   620	        ).into_iter().map(|name| {
   621	            let index = self.schema.tables[&name].expiry_index().expect("expiring table").0.to_owned();
   622	            (name, index)
   623	        }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   624	        'tables: for (table_name, index) in &expiring {
   625	            let mut bounds = due.clone();
   626	            bounds.after = expiry_after.get(table_name).cloned();
   627	            let mut wrapped = bounds.after.is_none();
   628	            loop {
   629	                let batch = (limit - visited).min(256);
   630	                if batch == 0 {
   631	                    break 'tables;
   632	                }
   633	                let rows = tx.scan(table_name, index, &[], &bounds, crate::store::Dir::Asc, batch)?;
   634	                let count = rows.len();
   635	                for (row, key) in rows {
   636	                    bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   637	                    if let Some(after) = &bounds.after { expiry_after.insert(table_name.clone(), after.clone()); }
   638	                    visited += 1;
   639	                    through_activation::mark_expiry_table(&mut expiry_after, table_name);
   640	                    if removed > 0 && started.elapsed() >= budget { break 'tables; }
   641	                    match runner.retire_expired_row(table_name, &row) {
   642	                        Ok(true) => removed += 1,
   643	                        Ok(false) => {},
   644	                        // An expected reference/read-through blocker retains
   645	                        // the logically expired root. Continue by key so it
   646	                        // cannot wedge cleanup of unrelated due rows.
   647	                        Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED" | "E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN", .. }) => {},
   648	                        Err(error) => return Err(error),
   649	                    }
   650	                }
   651	                if count < batch {
   652	                    expiry_after.remove(table_name);
   653	                    if !wrapped && visited < limit {
   654	                        bounds = due.clone();
   655	                        wrapped = true;
   656	                        continue;
   657	                    }
   658	                    break;
   659	                }
   660	            }
   661	        }
   662	        let events = runner.events.take();
   663	        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
   664	        let written = runner.rows_written;
   665	        drop(runner);
   666	        if written > 0 {
   667	            tx.record_commit(seq, now, "expire", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "sweep", &tables, written)?;
   668	            partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
   669	        }
   670	        tx.commit()?;
   671	        self.expiry_after = expiry_after;
   672	        self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
   673	        Ok(removed)
   674	    }
