   435	impl super::runner::Runner<'_> {
   436	    /// Authoritative adapters feed an ancestor-ordered batch to the ordinary
   437	    /// insert/update path; captures always resolve inside this transaction.
   438	    pub fn seed_server_rows(
   439	        &mut self,
   440	        rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>,
   441	    ) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   442	        if !self.context.is_system() || self.device {
   443	            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(
   444	                "server seed requires system authority".into(),
   445	            ));
   446	        }
   447	        let order = self.schema.through_order();
   448	        let mut rows: Vec<_> = rows.into_iter().collect();
   449	        rows.sort_by_key(|(n, _)| order.iter().position(|x| x == n));
   450	        let count = rows.len() as u64;
   451	        for (name, row) in rows {
   452	            super::timeline::check_import(self.schema, &name)?;
   453	            // Adoption rewrites are system imports of stored images (LLP 3000.015 §1).
   454	            self.upsert(
   455	                &name,
   456	                &row.field("id")?.clone(),
   457	                row.import_input(&super::ImportInput(())),
   458	            )?;
   459	        }
   460	        self.check_final_exists()?;
   461	        Ok(count)
   462	    }
   463	
   464	    /// A bounded child-first sweep on the common Rows interface.
   465	    pub fn sweep_server_rows(&mut self, limit: usize, expiry_after: &mut BTreeMap<String, Vec<Value>>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   466	        if !self.context.is_system() || self.device {
   467	            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(
   468	                "server sweep requires system authority".into(),
   469	            ));
   470	        }
   471	        let now = self
   472	            .context
   473	            .now
   474	            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("sweep clock is not pinned".into()))?;
   475	        let mut count = 0;
   476	        let mut visited = 0;
   477	        let tables = resume_expiry_tables(self.schema.through_order().into_iter().rev().collect(), expiry_after);
   478	        for name in tables {
   479	            let Some((index, _)) = self.schema.tables[&name].expiry_index() else {
   480	                continue;
   481	            };
   482	            let due = Bounds { gte: Some(Value::Int(i64::MIN)), lte: Some(Value::Int(now)), ..Default::default() };
   483	            let mut bounds = due.clone();
   484	            bounds.after = expiry_after.get(&name).cloned();
   485	            let mut wrapped = bounds.after.is_none();
   486	            while visited < limit {
   487	                let batch = (limit - visited).min(256);
   488	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
   489	                    runner
   490	                        .tx
   491	                        .scan(scope, &name, index, &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, batch)
   492	                })?;
   493	                if page.is_empty() {
   494	                    expiry_after.remove(&name);
   495	                    if !wrapped && visited < limit {
   496	                        bounds = due.clone();
   497	                        wrapped = true;
   498	                        continue;
   499	                    }
   500	                    break;
   501	                }
   502	                let page_len = page.len();
   503	                for (row, key) in page {
   504	                    bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   505	                    if let Some(after) = &bounds.after { expiry_after.insert(name.clone(), after.clone()); }
   506	                    visited += 1;
   507	                    mark_expiry_table(expiry_after, &name);
   508	                    match self.retire_expired_row(&name, &row) {
   509	                        Ok(true) => count += 1,
   510	                        Ok(false) => {},
   511	                        Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED" | "E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN", .. }) => {},
   512	                        Err(error) => return Err(error),
   513	                    }
   514	                }
   515	                if page_len < batch {
   516	                    expiry_after.remove(&name);
   517	                    if !wrapped && visited < limit {
   518	                        bounds = due.clone();
   519	                        wrapped = true;
   520	                        continue;
   521	                    }
   522	                    break;
   523	                }
   524	            }
   525	        }
   526	        Ok(count)
   527	    }
   528	}
     1	//! Referential actions on delete (LLP 3000.039 §3): before a delete removes
     2	//! its row, reserve a schema-fixed charge, close over the `cascade`
     3	//! references, refuse while a `restrict` reference still holds a closure row
     4	//! from outside the closure, clear `set null` references outside it, and
     5	//! remove the closure children first. The caller then removes the row itself.
     6	// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — closure first; probes exclude the closure; deletion dominates set null.
     7	use crate::engine::runner::Runner;
     8	use crate::{
     9	    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    10	    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    11	    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    12	    schema::{Effect, references::{OnDelete, Referencing}},
    13	    value::Value,
    14	};
    15	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
    16	
    17	impl Runner<'_> {
    18	    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    19	        let schema = self.schema;
    20	        if !schema.is_referenced(table_name) { return Ok(()); }
    21	        let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(table_name) else {
    22	            // A device without a reached child's exported price cannot charge
    23	            // what the server charges; the server always has every price.
    24	            if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} needs every referencing table's write price; queued without a local delete"))); }
    25	            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name}'s delete actions have no reservation: a cascading reference lacks its cap or its child's write price (guide writes)")));
    26	        };
    27	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.2 — what the commit publishes is fixed by the schema, not by the rows the actions reach.
    28	        let closure = schema.action_tables(table_name);
    29	        for table in &closure.written {
    30	            if schema.visible(table) { self.written_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
    31	            self.action_tables.insert(table.clone());
    32	        }
    33	        for table in &closure.read { self.read_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
    34	        if !closure.written.is_empty() { self.tx.widen_change_evidence(); }
    35	        for (kind, amount) in [(MeterKind::Steps, price[0]), (MeterKind::IndexEntries, price[1]), (MeterKind::Examined, price[2]), (MeterKind::RowsWritten, price[3]), (MeterKind::OutputBytes, price[5]), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, price[6])] {
    36	            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }
    37	        }
    38	        // Pending event images survive the closure and subsequent statements.
    39	        // Keep their schema-fixed reservation until the request ends.
    40	        let events = schema.delete_event_heap(table_name).unwrap_or(0);
    41	        self.meter.heap(events)?;
    42	        let mark = self.meter.mark();
    43	        self.meter.heap(price[4].saturating_sub(events))?;
    44	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — draw the reservation's rows from the request's physical allowance before the first seek.
    45	        if !self.context.is_system() { self.tx.draw(table_name, price[2])?; }
    46	        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort));
    47	        let prepaid = self.tx.reference_prepaid(true);
    48	        let action = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
    49	        let result = self.reference_closure(table_name, old);
    50	        self.engine_action = action;
    51	        self.tx.reference_prepaid(prepaid);
    52	        self.meter = meter;
    53	        self.meter.release_heap(mark);
    54	        result
    55	    }
    56	
    57	    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    58	    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    59	    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    60	        let schema = self.schema;
    61	        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
    62	        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
    63	        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
    64	        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
    65	        let mut next = 0;
    66	        while next < visits.len() {
    67	            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
    68	            next += 1;
    69	            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {

   143	    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
   144	    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
   145	    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
   146	    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
   147	    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
   148	    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
   149	    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   150	        let schema = self.schema;
   151	        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   152	        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
   153	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
   154	        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
   155	            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
   156	            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
   157	        } else {
   158	            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
   159	        };
   160	        let mut live = 0_u64;
   161	        for (prefix, gt) in ranges {
   162	            let mut bounds = Bounds { gt, ..Default::default() };
   163	            loop {
   164	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, r.child, &index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, page_rows))?;
   165	                let more = page.len() == page_rows;
   166	                if let Some((_, key)) = page.last() { bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key).map(|v| v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect()); }
   167	                let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(page.len());
   168	                for (row, _) in page {
   169	                    if self.device {
   170	                        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a device predicts only over current rows: retained history queues the delete, and only a live, current (or own provisional) row is a witness.
   171	                        // A retained, no longer current row is work the reservation does not
   172	                        // price and a witness the device cannot judge: the delete queues.
   173	                        if self.tx.retained_row(r.child, &row)? && !self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? {
   174	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting meets retained {} history that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   175	                        }
   176	                        if self.expired(r.table, &row) || !(self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? || self.is_provisional(r.child, &row)?) {
   177	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs a current image of every {} row that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   178	                        }
   179	                    } else if self.expired(r.table, &row) { continue; }
   180	                    live += 1;
   181	                    // Activation validates caps and every write keeps them; more than the cap
   182	                    // is a store that broke its own invariant, never a hidden-row count.
   183	                    if live > cap { return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} holds more rows than its cap for one {} row", r.child, r.column, r.column))); }
   184	                    ids.push(row.field("id")?.clone());
   185	                }
   186	                for child in ids {
   187	                    if !visit(self, child)? { return Ok(()); }
   188	                }
   189	                if !more { break; }
   190	            }
   191	        }
   192	        Ok(())
   193	    }

   195	    /// A device predicts only over a referencing group it holds completely.
   196	    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — the held-group proof of LLP 3000.007 §20.5, per closure row and reference.
   197	    fn prove_reference_held(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   198	        let index = self.schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   199	        let (child, key) = (r.child.to_owned(), [id.clone()]);
   200	        // Key-decided readability, and the group itself held (a rejoined or
   201	        // unacquired range is unknown even when the rule admits the viewer).
   202	        if self.device_work(|runner| runner.upsert_absence_proven(&child, &index, &key))? && self.tx.current_group(&child, &index, &key)? { return Ok(()); }
   203	        Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs every {} row that references it held; queued without a local delete", r.child)))
   204	    }

   206	    /// The engine's own write of a `set null` reference: no rule, every constraint.
   207	    fn clear_reference(&mut self, table: &str, column: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   208	        let mut new = old.clone();
   209	        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut new { fields.insert(column.to_owned(), Value::Null); }
   210	        let patch = crate::value::object([(column, Value::Null)]);
   211	        let outer = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
   212	        let result = self.target_attempt(table, |runner| {
   213	            runner.reserve_maintains(table, &runner.schema.tables[table])?;
   214	            runner.replace_authorized(table, old, new, true, &patch, 0)
   215	        });
   216	        self.engine_action = outer;
   217	        result
   218	    }
   219	
   220	    /// The engine's own delete of a closure row: no rule, its constraints, its
   221	    /// `read through` children's probe, maintains and partition events.
   222	    fn delete_reached(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   223	        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
   224	            runner.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
   225	            let table = &runner.schema.tables[table_name];
   226	            runner.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
   227	            runner.delete_row(table_name, old)
   228	        })
   229	    }
   230	}
   231	
   232	/// Rows a child scan holds per seek: the reservation's transient `4 × row`
   233	/// (LLP 3000.039 §5.1, `Schema::delete_reservation`).
   234	const PAGE: usize = crate::schema::references::REFERENCE_PAGE;
   235	
   236	fn through_reference(r: &Referencing<'_>) -> bool {
   237	    r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column)
   238	}
   239	
   240	/// The closure's rows, children before parents: a table goes first when no
   241	/// other reached table must be removed before it (the cascade and `read
   242	/// through` edges are acyclic by `check`), and within a table, later
   243	/// discoveries first.
   244	fn deletion_order(schema: &crate::schema::Schema, reached: &[(String, String)]) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
   245	    let tables: Vec<&str> = {
   246	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
   247	        for (table, _) in reached { if !seen.contains(&table.as_str()) { seen.push(table.as_str()); } }
   248	        seen
   249	    };
   250	    // edges: child table -> the reached tables it must be removed before
   251	    // (its cascade parents and its `read through` parent; LLP 3000.039 §3).
   252	    let mut edges: Vec<(&str, &str)> = Vec::new();
   253	    for &child in &tables {
   254	        for &parent in &tables {
   255	            if child != parent && schema.deletion_precedes(child, parent) { edges.push((child, parent)); }
   256	        }
   257	    }
   258	    let mut order: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   259	    let mut remaining = tables.clone();
   260	    while !remaining.is_empty() {
   261	        // A table no remaining table references can go now (its referencers are gone).
   262	        let position = remaining.iter().position(|t| !remaining.iter().any(|other| other != t && edges.contains(&(*other, *t))))
   263	            .unwrap_or(remaining.len() - 1);
   264	        order.push(remaining.remove(position));
   265	    }
   266	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(reached.len());
   267	    for table in order {
   268	        out.extend(reached.iter().rev().filter(|(t, _)| t == table).cloned());
   269	    }
   270	    out
   271	}

    92	    pub(super) fn physical_children(
    93	        &mut self,
    94	        parent: &str,
    95	        id: &Value,
    96	    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    97	        let children: Vec<_> = self
    98	            .schema
    99	            .children(parent)
   100	            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())
   101	            .collect();
   102	        // Reserve the complete schema-fixed probe set before its first seek.
   103	        // Never short-circuit on an occupied table, including at low ceilings.
   104	        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the seeks draw from the request's physical allowance; occupancy never selects the logical charge.
   105	        let heap = children.iter().map(|name| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[name])).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
   106	        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::Steps, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
   107	            let mut occupied = false;
   108	            for name in &children {
   109	                occupied |= !runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?.is_empty();
   110	            }
   111	            Ok(occupied)
   112	        })
   113	    }

   115	    // A deadline suffix skips an arbitrary expired backlog in one seek.

   118	        parent: &str,

   138	                let occupied = runner.device_work(|runner| runner.current_children(parent, id, &children))?;

   142	                let never = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone(), Value::Null], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?;
   143	                let finite = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds { gt: Some(Value::Int(now)), ..Default::default() }, Dir::Asc, 1)?;
   144	                if !never.is_empty() || !finite.is_empty() {
   145	                    return Err(runner.parent_has_children(parent));
   146	                }
   147	                // §3.3 forbids physical removal even when §6's live probe is empty.
   148	                // The author's implementation ruling keeps this case fail-closed.
   149	                if !runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?.is_empty() {
   150	                    return Err(runner.parent_has_children(parent));
   151	                }
   152	            }
   153	            Ok(())
   154	        })
   155	    }
   156	
   157	    pub(super) fn parent_has_children(&self, parent: &str) -> RuntimeError {
   158	        RuntimeError::Structural {
   159	            code: "E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN",
   160	            message: format!("{parent} at {} has stored children; delete its live read-through children and sweep expired children, then retry `delete {parent}[id]` (guide rules)", self.statement_site),
   161	        }
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — retained history cannot terminally refuse a
   165	    // current delete. Mismatched, expired or uncovered witnesses must queue.
   166	    fn current_children(&mut self, parent: &str, id: &Value, children: &[String]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   167	        if children.is_empty() { return Ok(false); }
   168	        let uncertain = || RuntimeError::Prediction("parent deletion needs complete matching current child coverage; queued without a row".into());
   169	        self.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   170	        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[parent]))?;

   156	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — if-absent's insert arm needs the same image, unique-population and cap proof as upsert.
   157	        // Upsert mints its insert id; if-absent may instead supply one.
   158	        // Only a freshly minted id can skip the primary-key absence proof.
   159	        let minted = self.context.minted_ids.contains(&id);
   160	        let proof = if !self.device { None } else if minted {
   161	            table.indexes.iter().find(|(name, index)| {
   162	                    name.as_str() != "byId" && index.unique && !index_values(index, &row).contains(&Value::Null)
   163	                })
   164	                .map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).or_else(|| {
   165	                    table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { .. })).then_some("byId")
   166	                })
   167	        } else { Some("byId") };
   168	        if if_absent && let Some(index) = proof.or(self.device.then_some("byId")) {
   169	            self.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row)?;
   170	        }
   171	        // An upsert has already resolved this exact address (or minted the id)
   172	        // and reserved both target proofs. Retiring an expired predecessor
   173	        // uses that same allowance, not a third read-rule reservation.
   174	        let collision_probes = if upsert_index.is_some() { 0 } else { super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read) };
   175	        let has_children = self.schema.children(table_name).next().is_some()
   176	            || self.schema.is_referenced(table_name);
   177	        let predecessor = self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, collision_probes)], |runner, allowance| {
   178	            let existing = runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, &id)?;
   179	            if let Some(row) = &existing {
   180	                if !runner.expired(table, row) { runner.visible_collision(table_name, row, Effect::Insert, "unique id")?; }
   181	                // @ref LLP 3000.014#3-a-child-lives-within-its-parent — an expired birth with physical
   182	                // children collides like a live birth: check the old image's authority under the same
   183	                // reservation, without treating expiry as denial. A non-reader never probes the children.
   184	                else if has_children && !runner.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, row)? {
   185	                    return Err(runner.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "unique id"));
   186	                }
   187	            }
   188	            Ok(existing)
   189	        })?;
   190	        if predecessor.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| self.expired(table, old)) && has_children {
   191	            let mark = self.meter.mark();
   192	            if self.physical_children(table_name, &Value::Id(id.clone()))? {
   193	                // The bounded probes have completed. Their occupancy is not
   194	                // disclosed by the collision's logical meter or heap peak.
   195	                self.meter.restore(mark);
   196	                return Err(if predecessor.is_some() {
   197	                    RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}"))
   198	                } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "parent identity has stored children") });
   199	            }
   200	        }
   201	        if let Some(existing) = &predecessor {
   202	            if !self.expired(table, &existing) {
   203	                return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}")));
   204	            }
   205	            // The expired predecessor is internal authorization work, not a
   206	            // disclosed examination (LLP 3000.015 §§1–2).
   207	            self.retire_expired_row(table_name, existing)?;
   208	        }
   209	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — an authored plain insert predicts unproved; a later refusal is then the server's
   210	        // to report. The engine's own timeline writes are not the author's prediction.
   211	        if let Some(index) = proof.filter(|_| {
   212	            !if_absent && upsert_index.is_none() && !self.unproved_write && self.timeline_authority.is_none()
   213	        }) {
   214	            // An earlier unknown fact already makes this prediction unproved.
   215	            // Otherwise the proof's own unknown fact is not the prediction's.
   400	        {
   401	            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "read rule");
   402	            return Err(self.no_write_target());
   403	        }
   404	        if !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Delete, None, &old)? {
   405	            return Err(if readable {
   406	                RuntimeError::Rule { table: table_name.to_owned(), effect: Effect::Delete }
   407	            } else { self.no_write_target() });
   408	        }
   409	        self.delete_authorized(table_name, old)
   410	    }
   411	
   412	    pub(super) fn delete_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   413	        let old = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
   414	            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, &old)?;
   415	            // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — the actions may have changed the root (a maintained column mirrored from its children): remove its current image.
   416	            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
   417	            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, &id))?.unwrap_or(old)
   418	        } else { old };
   419	        self.delete_row(table_name, old)
   420	    }
   421	
   422	    /// A delete's own removal of one row, its rules already decided: receipt
   423	    /// membership, the read-through child probe, constraints, maintains and
   424	    /// the partition event. A referential cascade removes each row this way.
   425	    pub(super) fn delete_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   426	        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
   427	        self.receipt_member_removed(table_name, &old)?;
   428	        self.refuse_live_children(table_name,old.field("id")?)?;
   429	        self.check_constraints(table_name, Some(&old), &old, Effect::Delete)?;
   430	        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
   431	            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, &old)
   432	        })?;
   433	        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(&old), None, entries)?;
   434	        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(&old), None, Origin::Source)
   435	    }
   436	
   437	    /// Remove a row the engine itself retires (an expired one): no rule and
   438	    /// no constraint stands between the engine and its own sweep, while
   439	    /// aggregates and partition events apply as for any delete.
   440	    pub(super) fn remove(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   441	        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
   442	        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   443	        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
   444	            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, old)
   445	        })?;
   446	        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(old), None, entries)?;
   447	        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(old), None, Origin::Source)
   448	    }
   449	
   450	    /// Physically retire one row whose upper lifetime ended. Expiry bypasses
   451	    /// the root's authored delete rule and constraints, but it does not bypass
   452	    /// the schema's declared effects on rows that reference the root. A
   453	    /// read-through child still retains the expired parent physically.
   454	    // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — expiry cleanup runs the same closure/restrict/set-null actions as an authored delete.
   455	    pub(super) fn retire_expired_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   456	        if self.physical_children(table_name, old.field("id")?)? { return Ok(false); }
   457	        let current = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
   458	            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, old)?;

   460	            // physical image so index and cap entries match what is stored.
   461	            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?;
   462	            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))?
   463	                .unwrap_or_else(|| old.clone())

   465	            old.clone()
   466	        };

   468	        Ok(true)
   469	    }
   470	
   471	    /// At a program commit's end, retire every synced row the commit changed
   472	    /// and every row it inserted whose lifetime has already ended: its final
   473	    /// image was never live at a commit boundary, so it leaves no row for a
   474	    /// later sweep to announce. Inside the commit the
   475	    /// row existed as written (an insert-then-update still works). Engine
   476	    /// bookkeeping, unmetered: the device's prediction does not do it, and
   477	    /// the program's metering is already recorded. Its work, returned, stays
   478	    /// linear in the rows retired: each aggregate row takes one net change
   479	    /// and each audience key is scanned for a live row once. Seeds and loads
   480	    /// keep their rows for the sweep.
   481	    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — a row whose lifetime ended by its own commit's end never exists at a commit boundary.
   482	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   483	    pub(crate) fn retire_expired_births(&mut self) -> Result<crate::meter::Metered, RuntimeError> {
   484	        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(Default::default());
   485	        };
   486	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#3 — an expired final image never grants a new recipient the row's id.
   487	        // A row that existed before the commit and ends it already expired is
   488	        // retired too: accounted from the partitions it was in at the commit's
   489	        // start, it revokes there and announces nothing to a partition it only
   490	        // entered with its expiring image.
   491	        let mut changed: Vec<(String, String)> = self.born_rows.keys()
   492	            .chain(self.touched_rows.keys())
   493	            .filter(|(table, _)| self.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t| t.is_expiring()))
   494	            .cloned().collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect();
   495	        if changed.is_empty() {
   496	            return Ok(Default::default());
   497	        }
   498	        // The log's order must not depend on a map's iteration order.
   499	        let order = self.schema.through_order();
   500	        changed.sort_by_key(|(name,id)| {
   501	            (std::cmp::Reverse(order.iter().position(|n| n == name)),id.clone(),
   502	            )
   503	        });
   504	        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::unbounded(), crate::meter::Cut::Abort));
   505	        self.retiring = Some(Default::default());
   506	        let retired = changed.iter().try_for_each(|(table_name, id)| {
   507	            let Some(row) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))? else { return Ok(());
   508	            };
   509	            let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   510	            if table.expired(&row, now) { self.retire_expired_row(table_name, &row).map(|_| ()) } else { Ok(()) }
   511	        });
   512	        let aggregates = self.retiring.take().map(|retiring| retiring.aggregates).unwrap_or_default();
   513	        let retired = retired.and_then(|()| self.apply_retired_aggregates(aggregates));
   514	        let work = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, meter).used;
   515	        retired.map(|()| work)
   516	    }
   517	
   518	    /// A column with a declared default takes it when the written row leaves
   519	    /// it out, before validation and before the asset facts, so the row the
   520	    /// rules and the constraints see is the complete one.
   521	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — omitted defaults use the clock and viewer of the host running the insert.
   522	    pub(super) fn with_defaults(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
   523	        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
   524	        if !table.defaults.keys().any(|name| {
   525	            row.as_object().is_ok_and(|fields| !fields.contains_key(name))
   526	        }) {
   527	            return Ok(row);
   528	        }
   529	        let row = row.defaults(table,self.context.now.ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Input("write clock is not pinned".into()))?,&self.context.viewer)?;
   530	        self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?;

+#[test]
+fn ungrouped_view_is_a_scalar_with_one_hidden_physical_group() {
+    let bound = compile(r#"view total: count posts
+  read <- allow
+  online only
+query totalPosts():
+  return total
+"#);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs[0].result, snapback4_lang::types::Ty::Int);
+    let maintain = &bound.schema.maintains[0];
+    assert!(maintain.by.is_empty());
+    let target = &bound.schema.tables["total"];
+    assert_eq!(target.columns.get("$global"), Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool));
+    assert_eq!(target.indexes["byGroup"].columns(), ["$global"]);
+    let encoded = serde_json::to_value(&bound.programs[0].program).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["index"], "byGroup");
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["key"], serde_json::json!([true]));
+}

new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..a589ba9349
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/wave12_semantics.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,381 @@
+use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
+use snapback4_lang::{compile, types::Ty};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+
+fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    bound
+}
+
+fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap()
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn global_count_is_zero_then_tracks_inserts_and_deletes() {
+    let source = r#"
+table entries:
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  delete <- allow
+  online only
+table moderators:
+  principal: principal
+  unique principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+view total: count entries
+  read <- allow
+  online only
+view privateTotal: count entries
+  read <- exists moderators[viewer]
+  online only
+mutation add(id: id):
+  return insert entries { id }
+mutation remove(id: entries):
+  delete entries[id]
+  return null
+query totalEntries():
+  return total
+query privateEntries():
+  return privateTotal
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    let generated = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound);
+    let native = snapback4_lang::emit::rust::rust(&bound);
+    assert!(!generated.contains("$global") && !native.contains("$global"),
+        "the physical discriminator leaked into generated application types");
+    for expression in ["total first 1 by .id", "total[true]"] {
+        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery bad(c: cursor ?):\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
+            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    let query = |engine: &mut Engine| engine.query("totalEntries", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    let private = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| engine.query("privateEntries", Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Null, "an unauthorized empty global stays hidden");
+    engine.seed([("moderators".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("mod".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(0), "authorized absence is the count identity");
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden absence is not zero");
+    for id in ["a", "b"] {
+        let outcome = engine.mutate("add", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id(id.into()))]))).unwrap();
+        assert!(matches!(outcome.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{outcome:?}");
+    }
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored scalar remains null");
+    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("a".into()))]))).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(1));
+    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("b".into()))]))).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
+    assert!(engine.store().rows("total").unwrap().is_empty(), "zero deletes the physical aggregate row");
+    assert!(engine.store().rows("privateTotal").unwrap().is_empty(), "private zero also has no physical row");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn matching_count_guard_narrows_only_the_same_required_projection_until_a_write() {
+    let schema = r#"
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  score: int
+  optionalScore: int ?
+  by byOwner: owner, id
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  delete <- allow
+  online only
+"#;
+    let sound = checked(&format!(r#"{schema}
+query stats(owner: principal):
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return {{ low: min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), high: max(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), total: sum(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) }}
+query filtered(owner: principal):
+  require count(entries where .owner = owner) > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries where .owner = owner, .score) + 1
+mutation freshAfterWrite(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#));
+    let result = &sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "stats").unwrap().result;
+    assert!(matches!(result, Ty::Object(fields) if fields.values().all(|ty| ty == &Ty::Int)), "{result:?}");
+    assert_eq!(sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "filtered").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+
+    for (name, body) in [
+        ("different", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries['other'] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("optional", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .optionalScore) + 1"),
+        ("written", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  delete entries[victim]\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("stale_binding", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  delete entries[victim]\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("rebound", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("joined", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  if flag:\n    n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("root_rebound", "who = owner\n  n = count(entries[who] by byOwner)\n  who = next\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[who] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+    ] {
+        let args = match name {
+            "written" | "stale_binding" => "owner: principal, victim: entries",
+            "joined" => "owner: principal, flag: bool",
+            "root_rebound" => "owner: principal, next: principal",
+            _ => "owner: principal",
+        };
+        let kind = if matches!(name, "written" | "stale_binding") { "mutation" } else { "query" };
+        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{schema}{kind} {name}({args}):\n  {body}\n"))]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{name}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+
+    let helper = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn remove(victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  return true
+mutation staleHelper(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  removed = remove(victim)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(helper.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{:#?}", helper.diagnostics);
+
+    let authority = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+job staleAuthority():
+  for row in entries:
+    n = count(entries[row.owner] by byOwner)
+    as row.owner:
+      require n > 0 else EMPTY
+      narrowed = min(entries[row.owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(authority.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{:#?}", authority.diagnostics);
+
+    let self_dependent = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), r#"
+table buckets:
+  bucket: int
+  score: int
+  by byBucket: bucket, id
+  public
+  online only
+query staleSelfDependent():
+  bucket = 1
+  bucket = count(buckets[bucket] by byBucket)
+  require bucket > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(buckets[bucket] by byBucket, .score) + 1
+"#.into())]);
+    assert!(self_dependent.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "a count result must not inherit the old value's domain provenance: {:#?}", self_dependent.diagnostics);
+
+    let pure_domain = checked(&format!(r#"{schema}
+fn sameOwner(owner: principal):
+  return owner
+query pureDomain(owner: principal):
+  n = count(entries[sameOwner(owner)] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[sameOwner(owner)] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#));
+    assert_eq!(pure_domain.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "pureDomain").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+
+    let impure_domain = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn changingOwner(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  return owner
+mutation staleDomain(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  n = count(entries[changingOwner(owner, victim)] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[changingOwner(owner, victim)] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(impure_domain.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "writeful domains must never be replayed as aggregate proof: {:#?}", impure_domain.diagnostics);
+
+    let shadowed = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn count(owner: principal):
+  return 1
+query shadowedCount(owner: principal):
+  n = count(owner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(shadowed.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "a user helper named count must not create builtin provenance: {:#?}", shadowed.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn matching_role_guard_narrows_only_the_authorized_keyed_total() {
+    let source = r#"
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  public
+  online only
+table staff:
+  principal: principal
+  role: enum('finance')
+  unique principal, role
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  delete <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+view totals: count entries by .owner
+  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
+  online only
+query raw(owner: principal):
+  return totals[owner]
+query guarded(owner: principal):
+  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance'] else NOT_FINANCE
+  return totals[owner] + 1
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "raw").unwrap().result, Ty::Int.optional());
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "guarded").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("one".into())), ("owner", Value::Id("bob".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    let raw = |engine: &mut Engine, owner: &str| engine.query("raw", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("owner".into(), Value::Id(owner.into())),
+    ]))).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored total is not disclosed");
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "nobody"), Value::Null, "a hidden miss is indistinguishable from a hidden hit");
+    engine.seed([("staff".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("finance".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
+        ("role", Value::String("finance".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Int(1));
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "nobody"), Value::Int(0), "an authorized keyed miss has the count identity");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("guarded", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("owner".into(), Value::Id("nobody".into())),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(1));
+
+    let stale = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{source}
+mutation stale(owner: principal, grant: staff):
+  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance'] else NOT_FINANCE
+  delete staff[grant]
+  return totals[owner] + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(stale.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
+        && diagnostic.message.contains("int ?")), "a write must invalidate the role proof: {:#?}", stale.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn email_format_is_one_shared_ascii_contract() {
+    let source = r#"
+table contacts:
+  email: text 3..254 format email
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  online only
+query echo(email: text 3..254 format email):
+  return email
+"#;
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    for email in ["a@example.com", "first.last+tag@sub.example", "x@localhost"] {
+        let result = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(result.data, Value::String(email.into()));
+    }
+    for email in ["a..b@example.com", ".a@example.com", "a@-example.com", "a@example..com", "a@пример.рф", "a@@example.com"] {
+        let error = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap_err();
+        assert_eq!(error.code, "E_INPUT", "{email}: {error:?}");
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn enum_columns_use_the_existing_word_search_path() {
+    let source = r#"
+table businesses:
+  category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')
+  search category
+  public
+  online only
+query find(q: text <=40, c: cursor ?):
+  return businesses matching q first 20 by .id after c
+"#;
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    engine.seed([
+        ("businesses".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", Value::Id("cafe".into())), ("category", Value::String("coffee-shop".into()))])),
+        ("businesses".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", Value::Id("books".into())), ("category", Value::String("book_store".into()))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let result = engine.query("find", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("q".into(), Value::String("cof".into())), ("c".into(), Value::Null),
+    ]))).unwrap();
+    let rows = result.data.as_array().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(rows[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id("cafe".into()));
+}

+#[test]
+fn rounded_division_is_exact_signed_and_keeps_numeric_types() {
+    let source = r#"
+query rounded():
+  return [
+    divide(5, 2, 'toward-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'away-from-zero'),
+    divide(5, 2, 'floor'), divide(5, 2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'half-even'),
+    divide(7, 2, 'half-even'), divide(-5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'),
+    divide(-5, 2, 'floor'), divide(-5, 2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(5, -2, 'floor'), divide(5, -2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(7, 3, 'half-even'), divide(8, 3, 'half-even')
+  ]
+query decimalValue(value: decimal 2):
+  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
+query moneyValue(value: money USD 2):
+  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
+query scaled(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return [
+    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-even'),
+    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-away-from-zero')
+  ]
+query wide(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return divide(numerator, denominator, 12, 'half-even')
+query scaleZero(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return divide(numerator, denominator, 0, 'half-even')
+query scaledOverflow():
+  return divide(9007199254740991, 1, 1, 'half-even')
+query zero():
+  return divide(1, 0, 'half-even')
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "decimalValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Decimal(2));
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "moneyValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Money("USD".into(), 2));
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    let values = engine.query("rounded", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(values, Value::Array([2, 3, 2, 3, 3, 2, 4, -3, -3, -2, -3, -2, 2, 3].into_iter().map(Value::Int).collect()));
+    for name in ["decimalValue", "moneyValue"] {
+        let value = engine.query(name, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("value".into(), Value::Int(5))]))).unwrap().data;
+        assert_eq!(value, Value::Int(2), "{name}");
+    }
+    let scaled = |engine: &mut Engine, numerator, denominator| engine.query("scaled", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(numerator)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(denominator)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(5050), Value::Int(5050)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-5050), Value::Int(-5050)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(12), Value::Int(13)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-12), Value::Int(-13)]));
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("wide", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
+        ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(1_000_000_000_000), "the wide scaled intermediate is not restricted to i53");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaleZero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(5)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(2)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaledOverflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("zero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_DIV_ZERO");
+
+    use snapback4_core::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Rounding, Stmt};
+    let overflow = Program {
+        name: "overflow".into(), kind: ProgramKind::Query, args: BTreeMap::new(),
+        read_time_granularity_ms: None, native: false, returned_shapes: BTreeMap::new(),
+        body: vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::Divide { rounding: Rounding::TowardZero, scale: None },
+            args: vec![Expr::Literal(Value::Int(i64::MIN)), Expr::Literal(Value::Int(-1))] })],
+    };
+    let mut raw = Engine::memory(Default::default(), [overflow]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(raw.query("overflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
+
+    for bad in ["divide(1, 2, 'nearest')", "divide(1, 2, mode)", "divide(1, divisor, 'half-even')",
+        "divide(1, 2, 13, 'half-even')", "divide(1, 2, scale, 'half-even')"] {
+        let source = if bad.contains("mode") { format!("query bad(mode: enum('half-even')):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else if bad.contains("scale") { format!("query bad(scale: int):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else if bad.contains("divisor") { format!("query bad(divisor: decimal 2):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else { format!("query bad():\n  return {bad}\n") };
+        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), source)]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"), "{bad}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+}

 fn run<H: Host>(host: &mut H) {
+    // Digest-addressed maintained rows use the same fixed identity on the
+    // server and each device store, even for the largest composite values in
+    // this generated family.
+    let aggregate = r#"
+table groups:
+  public
+  sync public
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  parent: groups
+  token: text <=64
+  amount: int
+  public
+  sync public
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token
+  public
+"#;
+    let (mut e, b) = compile(aggregate);
+    let principal = "p".repeat(64);
+    let parent = "r".repeat(64);
+    let token = "x".repeat(64);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("groups".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id(&parent))])),
+        ("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+            ("id", id("entry")), ("owner", id(&principal)), ("parent", id(&parent)),
+            ("token", snapback4_core::Value::String(token.clone())),
+            ("amount", snapback4_core::Value::Int(7)),
+        ])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[
+        id(&principal), id(&parent), snapback4_core::Value::String(token),
+    ]);
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+    let mut d = host.open("aggregate-digest", Some(b)).unwrap();

+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(rows[0].field("id").unwrap(), &id(&expected));

+#[test]
+fn aggregate_identity_generator_checks_bounded_digest_shape_outside_oracle_verdicts() {
+    // Maintained-view evaluation is outside the Lean oracle. Exercise its
+    // physical identity as an explicit generator invariant, so these cases
+    // cannot be counted as oracle passes merely because they are unsupported.
+    use snapback4_core::Value;
+    let mut rng = snapback4_difftest::generate::Rng::new(0x3000_0070_0030_0001);
+    let mut ids = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+    for n in 0..256 {
+        let key = if n == 0 {
+            vec![]
+        } else {
+            let principal = format!("p{:063}", rng.next());
+            let reference = format!("r{:063}", rng.next());
+            let len = rng.pick(65) as usize;
+            let token = format!("{:016x}", rng.next()).repeat(4)[..len].to_owned();
+            vec![Value::Id(principal), Value::Id(reference), Value::String(token),
+                 Value::Int((rng.next() & ((1 << 53) - 1)) as i64)]
+        };
+        let id = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&key);
+        assert_eq!(id.len(), 64, "seeded case {n}: {key:?}");
+        assert!(id.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_hexdigit() && !byte.is_ascii_uppercase()));
+        assert!(snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(&id));
+        assert_eq!(id, snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&key));
+        assert!(ids.insert(id), "unexpected digest collision in generated corpus");
+    }
+}

+#[test]
+fn generated_composite_aggregate_groups_keep_fixed_digest_identities() {
+    let source = r#"table parents:
+  insert <- allow
+  read <- allow
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  parent: parents
+  token: text <=64
+  amount: int
+  insert <- allow
+  read <- allow
+view totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token
+  read <- allow
+mutation add(row: id, owner: principal, parent: parents, token: text <=64):
+  insert entries {id: row, owner, parent, token, amount: 1}
+"#;
+    let mut e = engine(source);
+    let principal = "p".repeat(64);
+    let parent = "r".repeat(64);
+    e.seed([("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))]))]).unwrap();
+    for n in 0..32 {
+        let token = format!("{n:02}{}", "x".repeat(62));
+        let result = e.mutate("add", None, Context::new(&principal, BTreeMap::from([
+            ("row".into(), Value::Id(format!("e{n}"))),
+            ("owner".into(), Value::Id(principal.clone())),
+            ("parent".into(), Value::Id(parent.clone())),
+            ("token".into(), Value::String(token)),
+        ]))).unwrap();
+        assert!(matches!(result.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{result:?}");
+    }
+    let rows = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 32);
+    for row in rows {
+        let Value::Id(id) = row.field("id").unwrap() else { panic!("aggregate id") };
+        assert_eq!(id.len(), 64);
+        assert!(snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(id));
+        assert_eq!(id, &snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[
+            row.field("owner").unwrap().clone(), row.field("parent").unwrap().clone(),
+            row.field("token").unwrap().clone(),
+        ]));
+    }
+}

 ]

diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..241876980a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Maintained.lean
@@ -0,0 +1,67 @@
+import Quarry.Store
+
+/-!
+# Maintained aggregate identity
+
+The executable engine uses SHA-256 over a domain-separated canonical group
+tuple. This independent model deliberately does not reproduce that
+cryptographic implementation. It models the properties the language and
+store protocols rely on: versioned domain separation, a 64-character
+lowercase-hex identity for every logical group (including the empty tuple),
+and adoption as one state transition that replaces every legacy physical
+identity while publishing the new version.
+-/
+
+namespace Quarry
+
+/-- The protocol domain whose change requires another store capability and
+migration. -/
+def maintainedIdDomainV1 : List Nat := cps "snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1"
+
+/-- A small independent digest abstraction. Its contents are intentionally
+not the engine's SHA-256; only its versioned input and fixed hex shape belong
+to this logical model. -/
+def maintainedIdV1 (key : Key) : List Nat :=
+  (List.range 64).map fun i =>
+    let nibble := (maintainedIdDomainV1.length + (encKey key).length + i) % 16
+    if nibble < 10 then 48 + nibble else 87 + nibble
+
+theorem maintainedIdV1_length (key : Key) : (maintainedIdV1 key).length = 64 := by
+  simp [maintainedIdV1]
+
+/-- The canonical tagged-id overhead is nine bytes and lowercase ASCII is one
+byte per character, so every maintained identity fits the 144-byte id bound. -/
+theorem maintainedIdV1_fits (key : Key) : 9 + (maintainedIdV1 key).length ≤ 144 := by
+  rw [maintainedIdV1_length]
+  omega
+
+def maintainedRowV1 (key : Key) (cols : List (String × Scalar)) : Row :=
+  { id := maintainedIdV1 key, cols }
+
+structure MaintainedState where
+  version : Nat
+  rows : List Row
+
+/-- Adoption rebuilds all reconstructible aggregate rows and publishes the
+identity version in the same pure state result. `old` is an input so the model
+states explicitly that legacy physical ids are never retained. -/
+def adoptMaintainedV1 (_old : MaintainedState)
+    (groups : List (Key × List (String × Scalar))) : MaintainedState :=
+  { version := 1, rows := groups.map fun group => maintainedRowV1 group.1 group.2 }
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_version (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).version = 1 := rfl
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_ids (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.id = groups.map fun group => maintainedIdV1 group.1 := by
+  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
+
+theorem adoptMaintainedV1_values (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
+    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.cols = groups.map Prod.snd := by
+  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
+
+example : (maintainedIdV1 []).length = 64 := maintainedIdV1_length []
+example : (maintainedIdV1 [.id (List.replicate 64 97), .text (List.replicate 64 120)]).length = 64 :=
+  maintainedIdV1_length _
+
+end Quarry

 /-- **Write authority, whole mutation.** Every committed mutation's store is
 reached from the original by a chain of authorized steps. -/
 theorem mutation_authorized {c : Case} {res : Value} {s' : Store}
-    (hk : c.program.kind = .mutation) (h : run c = .committed res s') :
+    (hk : c.program.kind = .mutation) (hs : c.sweepOnly = false)

     Steps c.schema c.viewer c.now c.store s' := by
-  simp only [run, hk, runMutation] at h

   split at h

@@ -29,10 +29,10 @@ order, and its read rule was decided by the pins (`pageRead_complete`).
 namespace Quarry
 
 /-- **Noninterference.** -/
-theorem query_noninterference {c : Case} (hk : c.program.kind = .query) {s1 s2 : Store}
+theorem query_noninterference {c : Case} (hk : c.program.kind = .query) (hs : c.sweepOnly = false) {s1 s2 : Store}
     (h : visibleRowsAt c.schema s1 c.viewer c.now = visibleRowsAt c.schema s2 c.viewer c.now) :
     run { c with store := s1 } = run { c with store := s2 } := by
-  simp only [run, hk, runQuery, Case.ctx, h]
+  simp [run, hs, hk, runQuery, Case.ctx, h]
 
 /-- A query never changes anything: its world is the view, and every write
 refuses. -/
@@ -329,10 +329,10 @@ theorem visibleRows_insert_hidden {sch : Schema} {s : Store} {v : List Nat} {t :
   · simp only [Store.insertRow, Store.set, ht, ite_false]
 
 /-- **A hidden row changes no query outcome.** -/
-theorem query_ignores_hidden_insert {c : Case} (hk : c.program.kind = .query) {t : String} {r : Row}
+theorem query_ignores_hidden_insert {c : Case} (hk : c.program.kind = .query) (hs : c.sweepOnly = false) {t : String} {r : Row}
     (h : visibleRowsAt c.schema (c.store.insertRow t r) c.viewer c.now =
       visibleRowsAt c.schema c.store c.viewer c.now) :
     run { c with store := c.store.insertRow t r } = run c :=
-  query_noninterference (s2 := c.store) hk h
+  query_noninterference (s2 := c.store) hk hs h
 
 end Quarry

   program : Program
   args : List (String × Value) := []
   budget : Option Nat := none
+  /-- Run only the server expiry sweep and return its resulting store. -/
+  sweepOnly : Bool := false
 
 def Case.ctx (c : Case) : Ctx :=
   { schema := c.schema, viewer := c.viewer, now := c.now, helpers := c.helpers, budget := c.budget,
@@ -76,8 +78,9 @@ def runMutation (c : Case) : Outcome :=
     | .error .unavailable => .refused "E_BOUND"
 
 def run (c : Case) : Outcome :=
-  match c.program.kind with
-  | .query => runQuery c
-  | .mutation => runMutation c
+  if c.sweepOnly then .committed .null (sweepExpired c.schema c.store c.now)
+  else match c.program.kind with
+    | .query => runQuery c
+    | .mutation => runMutation c

diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Store.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Store.lean
index 629bc9ed03..38c1275945 100644
--- a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Store.lean
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Store.lean
@@ -215,11 +215,31 @@ def opened (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
     | .int opens => opens <= now
     | _ => false
 
-/-- The physical store with future rows removed. Rule probes use this store too,
-so an unopened row cannot act as an authorization witness. -/
+/-- Whether a row remains before its table's upper read lifetime. Null has no
+upper bound; the checked schema guarantees that a non-null value is a time. -/
+-- @ref LLP 3000.036.003#3-semantics — upper read lifetimes filter every logical read before physical cleanup.
+def unexpired (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
+  match td.expire with
+  | none => true
+  | some c => match r.get c with
+    | .null => true
+    | .int closes => now < closes
+    | _ => false
+
+def live (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool := opened td now r && unexpired td now r
+
+/-- Referential actions ignore expired child images but still govern a child
+whose lower read lifetime has not opened yet, matching the physical engine. -/
+def unexpiredStore (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store := fun t =>
+  match sch.table? t with
+  | some td => (s t).filter (unexpired td now)
+  | none => []
+
+/-- The physical store with rows outside their logical lifetime removed. Rule
+probes use this store too, so a future or expired row cannot authorize work. -/
 def openedStore (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store := fun t =>
   match sch.table? t with
-  | some td => (s t).filter (opened td now)
+  | some td => (s t).filter (live td now)
   | none => []
 
 /-- Read-rule visibility for one image at a request clock. The image may be a
@@ -227,7 +247,7 @@ physical write target, while every probe it performs sees only opened rows. -/
 def readableAt (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int)
     (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   match sch.table? t with
-  | some td => opened td now r && td.read.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { self := some r }
+  | some td => live td now r && td.read.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { self := some r }
   | none => false
 
 /-- Request-time visibility, including the server-enforced lower read lifetime. -/

@@ -102,6 +102,8 @@ structure TableDecl where
   caps : List CapDecl := []
   /-- A lower read lifetime: null or values at most the request clock are open. -/

diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean
index 66c7335f73..1c59ded584 100644
--- a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Write.lean
@@ -219,6 +219,44 @@ def refDelete (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop
   if restricted sch s cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
   else pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) (setNulls sch cl s))
 
+/-- Apply actions to the physical store using only the rows that are logically
+live as action witnesses. The root may itself be absent from `witnesses`. -/
+def refDeleteFrom (sch : Schema) (witnesses physical : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
+  let cl := closure sch witnesses (storeSize sch witnesses + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
+  if restricted sch witnesses cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
+  else
+    let physical := cl.foldl (fun s (parent, row) => (referencing sch parent).foldl (fun s (child, col, action) =>
+      if action == .setNull then
+        ((witnesses child).filter fun k => names k col row.id && !inClosure cl child k).foldl
+          (fun s k => s.replaceRow child k (clearCol k col)) s
+      else s) s) physical
+    pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) physical)
+
+/-! ## Expiry retirement (LLP 3000.036.003 §6; LLP 3000.039 §3) -/
+
+def expired (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
+  match td.expire with
+  | none => false
+  | some c => match r.get c with
+    | .int closes => closes <= now
+    | _ => false
+
+def stored (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
+  (s t).any fun current => current.id == r.id
+
+/-- Retire every due physical row. `restrict` retains that root but never
+stops later due keys; cascade and set-null are committed with the root. The
+model has no read-through retained-history rows, so every non-restricted due
+root can be retired immediately. -/
+def sweepExpired (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store :=
+  sch.tables.foldl (fun s td => (s td.name).foldl (fun s r =>
+    if expired td now r && stored s td.name r then
+      match refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s td.name r with
+      | .ok next => next
+      | .error (.refuse "E_REFERENCED") => s
+      | .error _ => s
+    else s) s) s
+
 /-- Apply one write to the store. -/
 def applyWrite (cx : Ctx) (s : Store) : RWrite → Except Stop (Value × Store)
   | .insert t fields ifAbsent =>

 | Values | `Quarry/Value.lean` | Scalars: null, bool, i53 int, text (as code points), nominal id, bytes. Values: scalars, arrays, rows. The one total order. A self-delimiting order-preserving key encoding. |
 | Syntax | `Quarry/Syntax.lean` | Schema: tables, typed nullable columns, unique keys with `else CODE`, read/insert/update/delete rules. Rules: allow, deny, `=`, `!=`, `exists t[..]` probes, and, or, not, over the row, old/next images and `viewer`. Expressions: arithmetic, comparison, and/or/not, `!` / `else`, `??`, conditionals, pure helper calls, arrays, records, point reads, pages, `count`, `exists`. Statements: `x = e`, `require`, `if`, `return`, and insert/upsert/update/delete. |

+| Maintained identity | `Quarry/Maintained.lean` | An independent logical abstraction of the versioned aggregate digest: every logical group, including the empty tuple, has a 64-character identity within the id bound; adoption replaces legacy ids and publishes the version in one state. It does not reproduce or verify SHA-256. |
 | Evaluator | `Quarry/Eval.lean` | `evalExpr` and `execStmts`: total and structural on fuel. The page walk, `complete` and `next`, counts and search `exists`; when a scan is decided (its pins, or a page guard: a grant or a pinned parent probe). Reads see only a `View` of visible rows. |
 | Writes | `Quarry/Write.lean` | Insert, upsert, update and delete, with rule checks and the documented refusal codes (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, `E_CONFLICT`, `E_CONSTRAINT`, `E_REFERENCED`, application codes). A delete applies its references' `on delete` actions (LLP 3000.039 §3): the read prerequisite, the cascade closure, restrict probes outside it, set null, removal. Caps are carried as unenforced declarations: the emitter admits a capped table only when the program never inserts into or updates it. |
 | Calendar | `Quarry/Calendar.lean` | The UTC builtins of LLP 3000.040 §2.2: `day`, `hour`, `bucket` as period ordinals; `month`, `year`, `monthStart`, `yearStart` over the proleptic Gregorian calendar, by a 400/100/4/1-year decomposition independent of the engine's algorithm. Unbounded `Int`; the evaluator refuses `E_OVERFLOW` outside i53. |
