   317	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
   318	#[cfg_attr(feature = "sqlite", derive(Deserialize))]
   319	#[serde(tag = "state", rename_all = "lowercase")]
   320	pub enum WriteState {
   321	    Sent { seq: u64, result: Value },
   322	    Failed { why: Refusal, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] current: Option<Value> },
   323	}
   324	
   325	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
   326	#[cfg_attr(feature = "sqlite", derive(Deserialize))]
   327	pub struct MutationOutcome {
   328	    /// A delivery report retained only a receipt; persists across replay.
   329	    #[serde(skip)]
   330	    pub receipt_only: bool,
   331	    #[serde(flatten)]
   332	    pub state: WriteState,
   333	    pub metered: Metered,
   334	    pub tables: Vec<String>,
   335	    /// Source/ordinary events only; private row-keyed recipient changes are not observable.
   336	    pub events: usize,
   337	    /// Whether this call applied the write, or found it already applied.
   338	    pub replayed: bool,
   339	}
   340	
   130	    }
   131	}
   132	
   133	impl Schema {
   134	    pub fn supported_capabilities() -> Vec<String> { CAPABILITIES.iter().map(|(name, _)| (*name).into()).collect() }
   135	    fn has_receipt_columns(&self) -> bool {
   136	        self.tables.iter().any(|(name, table)| table.maintained.keys().any(|field|
   137	            self.receipt_dependencies.get(name).is_some_and(|fields| fields.contains(field))))
   138	    }
   139	    pub fn required_capabilities(&self) -> BTreeSet<String> {
   140	        CAPABILITIES.iter().filter(|(_, needs)| needs(self)).map(|(name, _)| (*name).into()).collect()
   141	    }
   142	}
   143	
   144	impl Serialize for Schema {
   145	    fn serialize<S: Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {
   146	        let required = self.required_capabilities();
   147	        // The derive's field order, omissions and bytes are unchanged for
   148	        // ordinary schemas, including the serialized conformance corpus.
   149	        if required.is_empty() { return Wire::serialize(self, serializer); }
   150	        let mut wire = self.clone();
   151	        for table in wire.tables.values_mut() {
   152	            table.constraints.retain(|c| !matches!(c, Constraint::Recorded));
   153	        }
   154	        let mut carrier = wire.tables.first_entry().ok_or_else(||
   155	            serde::ser::Error::custom("E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY: required capabilities need a table carrier"))?;
   156	        carrier.get_mut().constraints.push(Constraint::Capabilities(required));
   157	        Wire::serialize(&wire, serializer)
   158	    }
   159	}
   160	
   161	impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Schema {
   162	    fn deserialize<D: Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {
   163	        // The private remote derive uses the ordinary
   164	        // streaming serde codec. No JSON-value or content-buffer replay.
   165	        let mut schema = Wire::deserialize(deserializer)?;
   166	        let mut declared = BTreeSet::new();
   167	        let mut carriers = 0;
   168	        let mut legacy = false;
   169	        for table in schema.tables.values_mut() {
   170	            let mut recorded = 0;
   171	            table.constraints.retain(|constraint| {
   172	                if let Constraint::Capabilities(names) = constraint {
   173	                    carriers += 1;
   174	                    declared.extend(names.iter().cloned());
   175	                    false
   176	                } else if matches!(constraint, Constraint::Recorded) {
   177	                    recorded += 1;
   178	                    false
   179	                } else { true }
   180	            });
   181	            if recorded > 1 || (recorded != 0 && table.recorded.is_empty()) {
   182	                return Err(serde::de::Error::custom("E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY: invalid legacy Recorded marker"));
   183	            }
   184	            if recorded != 0 { legacy = true; declared.insert("recorded-attribution".into()); }
   185	        }
   186	        for name in &declared {
   187	            if !CAPABILITIES.iter().any(|(known, _)| name == known) {
   188	                return Err(serde::de::Error::custom(format!("E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY: unsupported engine capability {name}; upgrade the engine/device")));
   189	            }
   190	        }
   191	        let mut required = schema.required_capabilities();
   192	        // @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — RY already serialized the dependency closure.
   193	        // RP stamps every emitted schema so old devices refuse its new prediction
   194	        // facts, but an RP reader must still open RY's kept backend/database.
   195	        // All other capabilities and the carrier shape remain exact.
   196	        let legacy_receipt_prediction = !declared.contains("receipt-derived-prediction-v1")
   197	            && required.remove("receipt-derived-prediction-v1");
   198	        // The price carrier can be absent in an old projection. Missing prices
   199	        // are fenced per program, rather than bricking a kept store.
   200	        for cap in ["source-write-reservations-v1", "source-write-reservations-v2"] {
   201	            if declared.contains(cap) { required.insert(cap.into()); }
   202	            else { required.remove(cap); }
   203	        }
   204	        // Stores written before the digest migration have no carrier for this
   205	        // capability. Admit that persisted schema for server-side rebuilding;
   206	        // its next serialization stamps the capability, so old devices refuse.
   207	        if !declared.contains("maintained-id-sha256-v1") {
   208	            required.remove("maintained-id-sha256-v1");
   209	        }
   210	        // Before the shared fence, the binder emitted Recorded on F2 tables
   211	        // even when the same schema also declared timelines. Only that legacy
   212	        // representation infers its full requirements; modern carriers remain
   213	        // exact, including when mixed with a legacy marker.
   214	        if legacy && carriers == 0 {
   215	            // A legacy marker only covers the capabilities that predate the fence.
   216	            // New semantics must never inherit permission from this escape hatch.
   217	            declared = required.iter().filter(|name| matches!(name.as_str(),
   218	                "maintained-column" | "recorded-attribution" | "timeline" | "timeline-v2")).cloned().collect();
   219	        }
   220	        if declared != required || carriers + usize::from(legacy) != usize::from(!required.is_empty()) {
   221	            return Err(serde::de::Error::custom(format!("E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY: schema requires {required:?}, but declares {declared:?} in {carriers} carriers")));
   222	        }
   223	        let had_prices = !schema.source_reservations.is_empty();
   224	        schema.prepare_receipt_dependencies();
   225	        // Opening an old blob preserves its wire shape. Complete visible
   226	        // declarations can still price a write on demand; hidden ones cannot.
   227	        if !had_prices { schema.source_reservations.clear(); }
   228	        schema.legacy_receipt_prediction = legacy_receipt_prediction
   229	            || (!declared.contains("source-write-reservations-v1") && !schema.receipts.is_empty());
   230	        Ok(schema)
   231	    }
   232	}

    63	/// Evaluation modes, not a claim of horizontally scalable commit ordering.
    64	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    65	pub enum WriteMode {
    66	    /// Reserve the head before executing, matching SQLite's single writer.
    67	    #[default]
    68	    Serialized,
    69	    /// Run concurrently, retrying the entire transaction on serialization
    70	    /// failure. A single global sync head is still a contention point.
    71	    Serializable,
    72	}
    73	
    74	pub(super) struct Store {
    75	    pub generation: u64,
    76	    pub client: Client,
    77	    cache: BTreeMap<&'static str, Statement>,
    78	    pub calls: Cell<u64>,
    79	    pub prefetch: bool,
    80	    pub marked_reads: Cell<u64>,
    81	    pub page_reads: Cell<u64>,
    82	    pub prefetch_rows: Cell<u64>,
    83	    pub prefetch_bytes: Cell<u64>,
    84	}
    85	
    86	impl Store {
    87	    pub fn new(client: Client) -> Self {
    88	        Self { generation:1, client, cache: BTreeMap::new(), calls: Cell::new(0), prefetch: true, marked_reads: Cell::new(0), page_reads: Cell::new(0), prefetch_rows: Cell::new(0), prefetch_bytes: Cell::new(0) }
    89	    }
    90	
    91	    fn begin(&mut self, mode: Option<WriteMode>) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
    92	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
    93	        self.page_reads.set(0);
    94	        let isolation = match mode {
    95	            None => IsolationLevel::RepeatableRead,
    96	            Some(WriteMode::Serialized) => IsolationLevel::ReadCommitted,
    97	            Some(WriteMode::Serializable) => IsolationLevel::Serializable,
    98	        };
    99	        let inner = self.client.build_transaction().isolation_level(isolation).read_only(mode.is_none()).start()?;
   100	        Ok(Tx { inner: RefCell::new(inner), cache: RefCell::new(&mut self.cache), calls: &self.calls, marked_reads: &self.marked_reads, page_reads: &self.page_reads, prefetch_rows: &self.prefetch_rows, prefetch_bytes: &self.prefetch_bytes,
   101	            rows_cache: RefCell::new(super::prefetch::Cache::default()), prefetch_enabled: self.prefetch, head: Cell::new(None), serialized_head: Cell::new(false), tail: RefCell::new(super::tail::Tail::default()) })
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    // Opening validates its backend and installs every physical upgrade before
   105	    // this transaction can commit. It cannot assume generation columns exist.
   106	    pub(super) fn opening_transaction(&mut self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   107	        self.begin(Some(WriteMode::Serialized))
   108	    }
   109	
   110	    pub fn transaction(&mut self, mode: Option<WriteMode>) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   111	        let generation_expected = self.generation;
   112	        let tx = self.begin(mode)?;
   113	        if mode == Some(WriteMode::Serialized) {
   114	            // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — a watermark covers commit order, not sequence allocation
   115	            let head = tx.query("SELECT seq FROM _head WHERE singleton FOR UPDATE", &[])?[0].get::<_, i64>(0) as u64;
   116	            tx.head.set(Some(head));
   117	            tx.serialized_head.set(true);
   118	        }
   119	        // A reader pins generation and watermark in the same snapshot and
   120	        // reuses this head for next_seq. A serialized writer checks generation
   121	        // after its head lock, so an adoption it waited for cannot be missed.
   122	        let generation = if mode.is_none() {
   123	            let row=tx.query("SELECT generation, (SELECT seq FROM _head WHERE singleton) FROM _backend WHERE singleton",&[])?.remove(0);
   124	            tx.head.set(Some(row.get::<_,i64>(1) as u64));row.get::<_,i64>(0) as u64
   125	        } else { tx.query("SELECT generation FROM _backend WHERE singleton",&[])?[0].get::<_,i64>(0) as u64 };
   126	        if generation != generation_expected {
   127	            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("another engine activated this store; reopen before reading or writing".into()));
   128	        }
   129	        Ok(tx)
   130	    }
   131	}

   135	    cache: RefCell<&'a mut BTreeMap<&'static str, Statement>>,

   145	    pub(super) serialized_head: Cell<bool>,

   149	pub(super) fn signed(n: u64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
   150	    i64::try_from(n).map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Store("Postgres evaluation integer exceeds BIGINT".into()))
   151	}
   152	pub(super) fn decode<T: serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(text: &str) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
   153	    serde_json::from_str(text).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))
   154	}
   155	pub(super) fn namespace(name: &str) -> Result<String, RuntimeError> {
   156	    if !name.starts_with("sb4_eval_") || name.len() > 63 || !name.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_lowercase() || b.is_ascii_digit() || b == b'_')
   157	    {
   158	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(
   159	            "evaluation schema must start with sb4_eval_ and contain only lowercase ASCII letters, digits, underscores (at most 63 bytes)"
   160	                .into(),
   161	        ));
   162	    }
   163	    Ok(format!("\"{name}\""))
   164	}
   165	
   166	impl Tx<'_> {
   167	    fn prepared(&self, sql: &'static str) -> Result<Statement, RuntimeError> {
   168	        if let Some(statement) = self.cache.borrow().get(sql) {
   169	            return Ok(statement.clone());
   170	        }
   171	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   172	        let statement = self.inner.borrow_mut().prepare(sql)?;
   173	        self.cache.borrow_mut().insert(sql, statement.clone());
   174	        Ok(statement)
   175	    }
   176	
   177	    pub fn query(&self, sql: &'static str, params: &[&(dyn ToSql + Sync)]) -> Result<Vec<Row>, RuntimeError> {
   178	        let statement = self.prepared(sql)?;
   179	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   180	        Ok(self.inner.borrow_mut().query(&statement, params)?)
   181	    }
   182	
   183	    pub fn execute(&self, sql: &'static str, params: &[&(dyn ToSql + Sync)]) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   184	        self.flush_tail()?;
   185	        let statement = self.prepared(sql)?;
   186	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   187	        Ok(self.inner.borrow_mut().execute(&statement, params)?)
   188	    }
   189	
   190	    pub fn batch(&self, sql: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   191	        if sql.to_ascii_uppercase().contains("ROLLBACK") {
   192	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store("use the explicit transaction/savepoint rollback method".into()));
   193	        }
   194	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   195	        self.inner.borrow_mut().batch_execute(sql)?;
   196	        Ok(())
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    pub fn rollback_program(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   200	        self.rows_cache.borrow_mut().clear();
   201	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   202	        self.inner.borrow_mut().batch_execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT program; RELEASE SAVEPOINT program")?;
   203	        Ok(())
   204	    }
   205	
   206	    pub(super) fn rollback_prefetch(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   207	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   208	        self.inner.borrow_mut().batch_execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT prefetch; RELEASE SAVEPOINT prefetch")?;
   209	        Ok(())
   210	    }
   211	
   212	    pub fn commit(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   213	        self.flush_tail()?;
   214	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   215	        self.inner.into_inner().commit()?;
   216	        Ok(())
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    pub fn rollback(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   220	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   221	        self.inner.into_inner().rollback()?;
   222	        Ok(())
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    pub fn record_commit(
   226	        &self,
   227	        seq: u64,
   228	        at: i64,
   229	        program: &str,
   230	        principal: &str,
   231	        kind: &str,
   232	        touched: &[String],
   233	        writes: u64,
   234	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   235	        self.tail.borrow_mut().commit = Some(super::tail::Commit {
   236	            seq, at, program: program.into(), principal: principal.into(), kind: kind.into(),
   237	            touched: canonical_json(&touched), writes,
   238	        });
   239	        Ok(())
   240	    }
   241	
   242	    pub fn applied(&self, principal: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
   243	        Ok(self.query("SELECT outcome FROM _ops WHERE principal = $1 AND id = $2", &[&principal, &id])?.first().map(|r| r.get(0)))
   244	    }
   245	
   246	    pub fn applied_bound(&self, principal: &str, id: &str, name: &str, args_digest: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
   247	        let Some(row) = self.query("SELECT outcome,name,args_digest FROM _ops WHERE principal = $1 AND id = $2", &[&principal, &id])?.into_iter().next() else { return Ok(None); };
   248	        if row.get::<_, &str>(1) != name || row.get::<_, &str>(2) != args_digest {
   249	            return Err(crate::store::id_reused());
   250	        }
   251	        Ok(Some(row.get(0)))
   252	    }
   253	
   254	    pub fn record_applied(&self, principal: &str, id: &str, seq: u64, outcome: &str, name: &str, args_digest: &str, at: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   255	        self.execute("INSERT INTO _ops(principal,id,seq,outcome,name,args_digest,at) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7)",
   256	            &[&principal, &id, &signed(seq)?, &outcome, &name, &args_digest, &at])?;
   257	        Ok(())
   258	    }

   260	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — this evaluation adapter records the emission at
//! Internal contribution format adoption under the evaluation adapter's writer lock.
use super::store::Tx;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}, schema::{IndexComponent, Schema, Table}, value::{Value, canonical_json}};

pub(super) fn install(tx: &Tx<'_>, schema: &mut Schema) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let backend = tx.query("SELECT schema,generation FROM _backend WHERE singleton", &[])?;
    let previous: Schema = super::store::decode(backend[0].get(0))?;
    let mut generation = backend[0].get::<_, i64>(1);
    schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
    if *schema != previous {
        for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
            for (index_name, index) in &table.indexes {
                let Some(IndexComponent::Contribution(maintain)) = index.components.first() else {continue};
                if previous.table(name).and_then(|t|t.index(index_name)) == Some(index) {continue;}
                tx.execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE t = $1 AND ix = $2", &[name,index_name])?;
                let work=adoption::Work::DECODE.with_contribution(maintain);
                let mut after: Option<String> = None;
                loop {
                    let mut meter=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);
                    meter.at(&format!("adopt {name}.{index_name}"));
                    meter.heap(4096 + after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
                    let page=source_page(&tx,name,table,after.as_deref(),&mut meter,work)?;
                    if page.is_empty() { break; }
                    for raw in &page {
                        let scratch=meter.mark();
                        let id=raw.get::<_,&str>(0);
                        let data=raw.get::<_,&str>(1);
                        meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)?;
                        adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
                        let row: Value=super::store::decode(data)?;
                        meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)?;
                        meter.step(maintain.evaluation_steps())?;
                        adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::contribution_bytes(&row,table))?;
                        for key in crate::rows::stored_index_keys(table,index,&row,id) {
                            if key.len() > crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES { return Err(crate::rows::index_key_refusal(name,index_name,1)); }
                            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries,1)?;
                            tx.execute("INSERT INTO i(t,ix,k,id) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4)", &[name,index_name,&key,&id])?;
                        }
                        drop(row);
                        meter.release_heap(scratch);
                    }
                    let last=page.last().expect("nonempty page").get::<_,&str>(0);
                    meter.heap(heap::text(last))?;
                    after=Some(last.to_owned());
                    // The full driver-owned page remains reserved until every
                    // decoded row and its index scratch have been consumed.
                    drop(page);
                }
            }
        }
        generation=generation.checked_add(1).ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?;
        tx.execute("UPDATE _backend SET schema = $1, generation = $2 WHERE singleton", &[&canonical_json(schema), &generation])?;
    }
    u64::try_from(generation).map_err(|_|RuntimeError::Generation("negative generation".into()))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9.3 — adoption reserves fetched images separately from per-row contribution scratch.
pub(super) fn source_page(tx: &super::store::Tx<'_>, table: &str, schema: &Table, after: Option<&str>, meter: &mut Meter, work: adoption::Work) -> Result<Vec<postgres::Row>, RuntimeError> {
    let sizes=if after.is_some() {
        "SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id) + octet_length(data)),0)::bigint, COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=$1 AND id>$2 ORDER BY id LIMIT $3) AS picked"
    } else {
        "SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id) + octet_length(data)),0)::bigint, COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=$1 AND $2::text IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT $3) AS picked"
    };
    let mut limit=32i64;
    let page_mark=meter.mark();
    loop {
        limit=work.limit(meter,limit as usize) as i64;
        let size=tx.query(sizes,&[&table,&after,&limit])?;
        let bytes=size[0].get::<_,i64>(0) as u64;
        let count=size[0].get::<_,i64>(1) as u64;
        // Network/read buffers, retained driver rows and their metadata. Typed
        // JSON decode is priced from borrowed text later, one row at a time;
        // the query scan's conservative 256x all-rows decoder is inappropriate
        // for this walk. This initial estimate is followed by a scratch check
        // before any adoption callback runs.
        let reservation=bytes.saturating_mul(8).saturating_add(count.saturating_mul(1024));
        if limit>1 && reservation>meter.ceilings.heap_bytes/2 { limit/=2; continue; }
        meter.heap(reservation)?;
        let query=if after.is_some() {
            "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=$1 AND id>$2 ORDER BY id LIMIT $3"
        } else {
            "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=$1 AND $2::text IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT $3"
        };
        let page=tx.query(query,&[&table,&after,&limit])?;
        if adoption::page_fits(page.iter().map(|raw| raw.get::<_,&str>(1)), meter, work,
            Some(&|row| adoption::contribution_bytes(row,schema)))? { return Ok(page); }
        limit=(page.len() as i64/2).max(1);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
    }
}

     1	import Quarry.Eval
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Writes: rule checks and refusals
     5	
     6	From the rules card, `why E_RULE`, `why NOT_FOUND`, `why E_CONFLICT` and the
     7	writes card:
     8	
     9	* **insert** — the insert rule judges the new image (`E_RULE`); a clash on
    10	  `id` or a declared unique key with a hidden row is `E_RULE`; with a visible
    11	  row, `if absent` returns null and an ordinary insert refuses `E_CONSTRAINT`
    12	  or the key's `else CODE`.
    13	* **update** — "readable" means the read rule admits the old row before the
    14	  write; the update rule judges old and next. Denial is `E_RULE` on a readable
    15	  target and `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable one; a missing target is
    16	  `NOT_FOUND`; an `if` condition on an unreadable target is `NOT_FOUND`, and a
    17	  false one on a readable target `E_CONFLICT`; a secondary unique clash is
    18	  `NOT_FOUND` (unreadable target), `E_RULE` (hidden colliding row) or
    19	  `E_CONSTRAINT`. An admitted update may change a hidden row; the result is
    20	  the row if it is readable afterwards, else null.
    21	* **upsert** — patches an existing target as an update; inserts an absent
    22	  one, where an incomplete image or insert-rule denial is `NOT_FOUND`.
    23	* **delete** — `NOT_FOUND` for a missing or unreadable refused target,
    24	  `E_RULE` for a readable one whose delete rule refuses. A row that
    25	  `restrict`, `cascade` or `set null` references name also needs read
    26	  authority (`NOT_FOUND`); then its delete closes over `cascade` references,
    27	  refuses `E_REFERENCED` while a `restrict` reference names a closure row
    28	  from outside the closure, clears `set null` references outside it, and
    29	  removes the closure (LLP 3000.039 §3).
    30	
    31	The order in which checks run when several fail is not documented; the
    32	model's order is OPEN W1–W6.
    33	-/
    34	
    35	namespace Quarry
    36	
    37	def notFound : Stop := .refuse "NOT_FOUND"
    38	def eRule : Stop := .refuse "E_RULE"
    39	def eInput : Stop := .refuse "E_INPUT"
    40	
    41	def toScalarE : Value → Except Stop Scalar
    42	  | .s x => pure x
    43	  | _ => throw eInput
    44	
    45	def scalarsE (fs : List (String × Value)) : Except Stop (List (String × Scalar)) :=
    46	  fs.mapM fun p => do return (p.1, ← toScalarE p.2)
    47	
    48	/-- A column value fits its declaration: only a nullable column takes null. -/
    49	def fits (c : ColDecl) (x : Scalar) : Bool := c.nullable || x != .null
    50	
    51	/-- The new image of an insert. An absent nullable column is null; an absent
    52	required column refuses with `missing`. Unknown fields refuse `E_INPUT`. -/
    53	def buildImage (td : TableDecl) (rid : List Nat) (fields : List (String × Scalar))
    54	    (missing : Stop) : Except Stop Row := do
    55	  if fields.any fun p => p.1 != "id" && !(td.cols.any (·.name == p.1)) then throw eInput
    56	  let cols ← td.cols.mapM fun c => match fields.lookup c.name with
    57	    | some x => if fits c x then pure (c.name, x) else throw eInput
    58	    | none => if c.nullable then pure (c.name, Scalar.null) else throw missing
    59	  return { id := rid, cols }
    60	
    61	/-- The next image of an update: the patch replaces named columns. -/
    62	def patchImage (td : TableDecl) (old : Row) (patch : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop Row := do
    63	  if patch.any fun p => !(td.cols.any (·.name == p.1)) then throw eInput
    64	  let cols ← old.cols.mapM fun p => match patch.lookup p.1 with
    65	    | some x =>
    66	      match td.cols.find? (·.name == p.1) with
    67	      | some c => if fits c x then pure (p.1, x) else throw eInput
    68	      | none => throw eInput
    69	    | none => pure p
    70	  return { old with cols }
    71	
    72	def keyOf (cols : List String) (r : Row) : List (String × Scalar) := cols.map fun c => (c, r.get c)
    73	
    74	/-- The first unique key (in order) on which another row (not `except`)
    75	collides with `img`. Keys with a null component never collide (a nullable
    76	unique key permits tied nulls; indexes card). -/
    77	def findClash (rows : List Row) (keys : List (List String)) (img : Row) (except : Option (List Nat)) :
    78	    Option (Nat × Row) :=
    79	  let rec go (i : Nat) : List (List String) → Option (Nat × Row)
    80	    | [] => none
    81	    | k :: ks =>
    82	      let kv := keyOf k img
    83	      if kv.any (·.2 == .null) then go (i + 1) ks
    84	      else match rows.find? (fun r => keyMatch kv r && except != some r.id) with
    85	        | some r => some (i, r)
    86	        | none => go (i + 1) ks
    87	  go 0 keys
    88	
    89	/-- The refusal for a visible clash on unique key `i` of `["id"] ++ uniques`
    90	(`withId`) or of `uniques`. -/
    91	def clashCode (td : TableDecl) (withId : Bool) (i : Nat) : Stop :=
    92	  let j := if withId then i - 1 else i
    93	  if withId && i = 0 then .refuse "E_CONSTRAINT"
    94	  else match td.uniqueCodes[j]? with
    95	    | some (some code) => .refuse code
    96	    | _ => .refuse "E_UNIQUE"
    97	
    98	def findRow (rows : List Row) (ks : List (String × Scalar)) : Option Row :=
    99	  if ks.any (·.2 == .null) then none else rows.find? (keyMatch ks)
   100	
   101	/-- The result of a write: the row if the viewer can read it afterwards,
   102	else null (`why E_CONFLICT`: results are read-rule gets; OPEN W4 for insert). -/
   103	def resultOf (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (t : String) (r : Row) : Value :=
   104	  if readable sch s v t r then r.toValue else .null
   105	
   106	def resultOfAt (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int)
   107	    (t : String) (r : Row) : Value :=
   108	  if readableAt sch s v now t r then r.toValue else .null
   109	
   110	/-- `guardE b e` continues when `b` holds and refuses `e` otherwise. -/
   111	def guardE (b : Bool) (e : Stop) : Except Stop Unit := if b then .ok () else .error e
   112	
   113	def doInsertImage (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   114	    (img : Row) (ifAbsent : Bool) (deny clashHidden : Stop) :
   115	    Except Stop (Value × Store) := do
   116	  guardE (td.insert.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { self := some img }) deny
   117	  match findClash (s t) (["id"] :: td.uniques) img none with
   118	  | some (i, r) => do
   119	    guardE (readableAt sch s v now t r) clashHidden
   120	    if ifAbsent then pure (.null, s) else throw (clashCode td true i)
   121	  | none =>
   122	    let s' := s.insertRow t img
   123	    pure (resultOfAt sch s' v now t img, s')
   124	
   125	/-- An update's `if` condition: `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable target,
   126	`E_CONFLICT` when false. -/
   127	def condCheck (rd : Bool) (cond : Option (Row → Except Stop Bool)) (old : Row) : Except Stop Unit :=
   128	  match cond with
   129	  | some f => do
   130	    guardE rd notFound
   131	    let b ← f old
   132	    guardE b (.refuse "E_CONFLICT")
   133	  | none => pure ()
   134	
   135	/-- A secondary unique clash of an update's next image with another row. -/
   136	def clashCheck (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   137	    (rd : Bool) (old nw : Row) : Except Stop Unit :=
   138	  match findClash (s t) td.uniques nw (some old.id) with
   139	  | some (i, r) =>
   140	    throw (if !rd then notFound else if !(readableAt sch s v now t r) then eRule else clashCode td false i)
   141	  | none => pure ()
   142	
   143	def doUpdateRow (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (now : Int) (t : String) (td : TableDecl)
   144	    (old : Row) (patch : List (String × Scalar)) (cond : Option (Row → Except Stop Bool)) :
   145	    Except Stop (Value × Store) := do
   146	  let nw ← patchImage td old patch
   147	  guardE (td.update.eval (openedStore sch s now) v { old := some old, next := some nw })
   148	    (if readableAt sch s v now t old then eRule else notFound)
   149	  condCheck (readableAt sch s v now t old) cond old
   150	  clashCheck sch s v now t td (readableAt sch s v now t old) old nw
   151	  pure (resultOfAt sch (s.replaceRow t old nw) v now t nw, s.replaceRow t old nw)
   152	
   153	/-- The id of an absent upsert's new row: from the key, else the patch. -/
   154	def upsertId (ks ps : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop (List Nat) :=
   155	  match ks.lookup "id" with
   156	  | some (.id x) => pure x
   157	  | _ => match ps.lookup "id" with
   158	    | some (.id x) => pure x
   159	    | _ => throw eInput
   160	
   161	def insertId (fs : List (String × Scalar)) : Except Stop (List Nat) :=
   162	  match fs.lookup "id" with
   163	  | some (.id x) => pure x
   164	  | _ => throw eInput
   165	
   166	/-! ## Referential actions (LLP 3000.039 §3) -/
   167	
   168	/-- Every reference that names table `t` and carries an action:
   169	`(child table, column, action)`. -/
   170	def referencing (sch : Schema) (t : String) : List (String × String × OnDelete) :=
   171	  sch.tables.flatMap fun td => td.cols.filterMap fun c =>
   172	    match c.ref, c.onDelete with
   173	    | some p, some a => if p == t then some (td.name, c.name, a) else none
   174	    | _, _ => none
   175	
   176	/-- Row `k` names the row with id `i` through column `c`. -/
   177	def names (k : Row) (c : String) (i : List Nat) : Bool := k.get c == .id i
   178	
   179	def inClosure (cl : List (String × Row)) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   180	  cl.any fun p => p.1 == t && p.2.id == r.id
   181	
   182	/-- The rows one step of cascades reaches from `(t, r)`. -/
   183	def cascadeKids (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : List (String × Row) :=
   184	  (referencing sch t).flatMap fun (c, col, a) =>
   185	    if a == .cascade then ((s c).filter fun k => names k col r.id).map fun k => (c, k) else []
   186	
   187	/-- Breadth-first closure over `cascade` references, each row once; `fuel`
   188	bounds the walk (the store's row count suffices). -/
   189	def closure (sch : Schema) (s : Store) : Nat → List (String × Row) → List (String × Row) → List (String × Row)
   190	  | 0, _, acc => acc
   191	  | _ + 1, [], acc => acc
   192	  | f + 1, (t, r) :: rest, acc =>
   193	    let new := (cascadeKids sch s t r).foldl (fun n p => if inClosure (acc ++ n) p.1 p.2 then n else n ++ [p]) []
   194	    closure sch s f (rest ++ new) (acc ++ new)
   195	
   196	/-- Some `restrict` reference names a closure row from outside the closure. -/
   197	def restricted (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (cl : List (String × Row)) : Bool :=
   198	  cl.any fun (t, r) => (referencing sch t).any fun (c, col, a) =>
   199	    a == .restrict && (s c).any fun k => names k col r.id && !inClosure cl c k
   200	
   201	def clearCol (r : Row) (c : String) : Row :=
   202	  { r with cols := r.cols.map fun p => if p.1 == c then (p.1, Scalar.null) else p }
   203	
   204	/-- Clear every `set null` reference that names a closure row from outside it. -/
   205	def setNulls (sch : Schema) (cl : List (String × Row)) (s : Store) : Store :=
   206	  cl.foldl (fun s (t, r) => (referencing sch t).foldl (fun s (c, col, a) =>
   207	    if a == .setNull then
   208	      ((s c).filter fun k => names k col r.id && !inClosure cl c k).foldl
   209	        (fun s k => s.replaceRow c k (clearCol k col)) s
   210	    else s) s) s
   211	
   212	def storeSize (sch : Schema) (s : Store) : Nat := sch.tables.foldl (fun n td => n + (s td.name).length) 0
   213	
   214	/-- The store after deleting `old` from `t` with its referential actions, or
   215	`E_REFERENCED`. With no action-carrying reference into `t`, exactly
   216	`removeRow` (LLP 3000.039 §1). -/
   217	def refDelete (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
   218	  let cl := closure sch s (storeSize sch s + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
   219	  if restricted sch s cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
   220	  else pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) (setNulls sch cl s))
   221	
   222	/-- Apply actions to the physical store using only the rows that are logically
   223	live as action witnesses. The root may itself be absent from `witnesses`. -/
   224	def refDeleteFrom (sch : Schema) (witnesses physical : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Except Stop Store :=
   225	  let cl := closure sch witnesses (storeSize sch witnesses + 1) [(t, old)] [(t, old)]
   226	  if restricted sch witnesses cl then throw (.refuse "E_REFERENCED")
   227	  else
   228	    let physical := cl.foldl (fun s (parent, row) => (referencing sch parent).foldl (fun s (child, col, action) =>
   229	      if action == .setNull then
   230	        ((witnesses child).filter fun k => names k col row.id && !inClosure cl child k).foldl
   231	          (fun s witness => match (s child).find? fun current => current.id == witness.id with
   232	            | some current =>
   233	              if names current col row.id then s.replaceRow child current (clearCol current col) else s
   234	            | none => s) s
   235	      else s) s) physical
   236	    pure (cl.foldl (fun s p => s.removeRow p.1 p.2) physical)
   237	
   238	/-! ## Expiry retirement (LLP 3000.036.003 §6; LLP 3000.039 §3) -/
   239	
   240	def expired (td : TableDecl) (now : Int) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   241	  match td.expire with
   242	  | none => false
   243	  | some c => match r.get c with
   244	    | .int closes => closes <= now
   245	    | _ => false
   246	
   247	def stored (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : Bool :=
   248	  (s t).any fun current => current.id == r.id
   249	
   250	/-- Retire every due physical row. `restrict` retains that root but never
   251	stops later due keys; cascade and set-null are committed with the root. The
   252	model has no read-through retained-history rows, so every non-restricted due
   253	root can be retired immediately. -/
   254	def sweepExpired (sch : Schema) (s : Store) (now : Int) : Store :=
   255	  sch.tables.foldl (fun s td => (s td.name).foldl (fun s r =>
   256	    if expired td now r && stored s td.name r then
   257	      match refDeleteFrom sch (unexpiredStore sch s now) s td.name r with
   258	      | .ok next => next
   259	      | .error (.refuse "E_REFERENCED") => s
   260	      | .error _ => s
   261	    else s) s) s
   262	
   263	/-- Apply one write to the store. -/
   264	def applyWrite (cx : Ctx) (s : Store) : RWrite → Except Stop (Value × Store)
   265	  | .insert t fields ifAbsent =>
   266	    match cx.schema.table? t with
   267	    | none => throw eInput
   268	    | some td => do
   269	      let fs ← scalarsE fields
   270	      let rid ← insertId fs
   271	      let img ← buildImage td rid fs eInput
   272	      doInsertImage cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t td img ifAbsent eRule eRule
   273	  | .update t key patch cond =>
   274	    match cx.schema.table? t with
   275	    | none => throw eInput
   276	    | some td => do
   277	      let ks ← scalarsE key
   278	      let ps ← scalarsE patch
   279	      match findRow (s t) ks with
   280	      | none => throw notFound
   281	      | some old => doUpdateRow cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t td old ps cond
   282	  | .upsert t key patch =>
   283	    match cx.schema.table? t with
   284	    | none => throw eInput
   285	    | some td => do
   286	      let ks ← scalarsE key
   287	      let ps ← scalarsE patch
   288	      match findRow (s t) ks with
   289	      | some old => doUpdateRow cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t td old ps none
   290	      | none => do
   291	        let rid ← upsertId ks ps
   292	        let img ← buildImage td rid (ks ++ ps) notFound
   293	        doInsertImage cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t td img false notFound notFound
   294	  | .delete t key =>
   295	    match cx.schema.table? t with
   296	    | none => throw eInput
   297	    | some td => do
   298	      let ks ← scalarsE key
   299	      match findRow (s t) ks with
   300	      | none => throw notFound
   301	      | some old => do
   302	        guardE (td.delete.eval (openedStore cx.schema s cx.now) cx.viewer { self := some old })
   303	          (if readableAt cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t old then eRule else notFound)
   304	        guardE ((referencing cx.schema t).isEmpty || readableAt cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now t old) notFound
   305	        pure (.null, ← refDelete cx.schema s t old)
   306	
   307	end Quarry
     1	import Quarry.Store
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Maintained aggregate identity
     5	
     6	The executable engine uses SHA-256 over a domain-separated canonical group
     7	tuple. This independent model deliberately does not reproduce that
     8	cryptographic implementation. It models the properties the language and
     9	store protocols rely on: versioned domain separation, a 64-character
    10	lowercase-hex identity for every logical group (including the empty tuple),
    11	and adoption as one state transition that replaces every legacy physical
    12	identity while publishing the new version.
    13	-/
    14	
    15	namespace Quarry
    16	
    17	/-- The protocol domain whose change requires another store capability and
    18	migration. -/
    19	def maintainedIdDomainV1 : List Nat := cps "snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1"
    20	
    21	/-- A small independent digest abstraction. Its contents are intentionally
    22	not the engine's SHA-256; only its versioned input and fixed hex shape belong
    23	to this logical model. -/
    24	def maintainedIdV1 (key : Key) : List Nat :=
    25	  (List.range 64).map fun i =>
    26	    let nibble := (maintainedIdDomainV1.length + (encKey key).length + i) % 16
    27	    if nibble < 10 then 48 + nibble else 87 + nibble
    28	
    29	theorem maintainedIdV1_length (key : Key) : (maintainedIdV1 key).length = 64 := by
    30	  simp [maintainedIdV1]
    31	
    32	/-- The canonical tagged-id overhead is nine bytes and lowercase ASCII is one
    33	byte per character, so every maintained identity fits the 144-byte id bound. -/
    34	theorem maintainedIdV1_fits (key : Key) : 9 + (maintainedIdV1 key).length ≤ 144 := by
    35	  rw [maintainedIdV1_length]
    36	  omega
    37	
    38	def maintainedRowV1 (key : Key) (cols : List (String × Scalar)) : Row :=
    39	  { id := maintainedIdV1 key, cols }
    40	
    41	structure MaintainedState where
    42	  version : Nat
    43	  rows : List Row
    44	
    45	/-- Adoption rebuilds all reconstructible aggregate rows and publishes the
    46	identity version in the same pure state result. `old` is an input so the model
    47	states explicitly that legacy physical ids are never retained. -/
    48	def adoptMaintainedV1 (_old : MaintainedState)
    49	    (groups : List (Key × List (String × Scalar))) : MaintainedState :=
    50	  { version := 1, rows := groups.map fun group => maintainedRowV1 group.1 group.2 }
    51	
    52	theorem adoptMaintainedV1_version (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
    53	    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).version = 1 := rfl
    54	
    55	theorem adoptMaintainedV1_ids (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
    56	    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.id = groups.map fun group => maintainedIdV1 group.1 := by
    57	  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
    58	
    59	theorem adoptMaintainedV1_values (old : MaintainedState) (groups) :
    60	    (adoptMaintainedV1 old groups).rows.map Row.cols = groups.map Prod.snd := by
    61	  simp [adoptMaintainedV1, maintainedRowV1]
    62	
    63	example : (maintainedIdV1 []).length = 64 := maintainedIdV1_length []
    64	example : (maintainedIdV1 [.id (List.replicate 64 97), .text (List.replicate 64 120)]).length = 64 :=
    65	  maintainedIdV1_length _
    66	
    67	end Quarry
     1	import Quarry.Syntax
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Rows, stores, rules and visibility
     5	
     6	A store maps each table name to its rows. Rules are evaluated against the
     7	whole store: an `exists t[..]` probe is an exact-key existence test that does
     8	not consult `t`'s own read rule (OPEN R1). A row is *visible* to a viewer
     9	when its table's read rule holds for it; every read the evaluator performs
    10	goes through `visibleRows`, so the read semantics in `Eval.lean` never sees
    11	a hidden row (`Disclosure.lean`).
    12	-/
    13	
    14	namespace Quarry
    15	
    16	structure Row where
    17	  id : List Nat
    18	  cols : List (String × Scalar)
    19	  deriving Repr, DecidableEq, Inhabited
    20	
    21	def Row.get (r : Row) (c : String) : Scalar :=
    22	  if c = "id" then .id r.id else (r.cols.lookup c).getD .null
    23	
    24	def Row.toValue (r : Row) : Value :=
    25	  .row (("id", .s (.id r.id)) :: r.cols.map fun p => (p.1, .s p.2))
    26	
    27	/-- The store: the rows of each table. -/
    28	abbrev Store := String → List Row
    29	
    30	def Store.empty : Store := fun _ => []
    31	
    32	def Store.set (s : Store) (t : String) (rs : List Row) : Store :=
    33	  fun t' => if t' = t then rs else s t'
    34	
    35	def Store.insertRow (s : Store) (t : String) (r : Row) : Store := s.set t (s t ++ [r])
    36	def Store.replaceRow (s : Store) (t : String) (old nw : Row) : Store :=
    37	  s.set t ((s t).map fun r => if r.id = old.id then nw else r)
    38	def Store.removeRow (s : Store) (t : String) (old : Row) : Store :=
    39	  s.set t ((s t).filter fun r => r.id ≠ old.id)
    40	
    41	/-- The images a rule may name. -/
    42	structure Imgs where
    43	  self : Option Row := none
    44	  old : Option Row := none
    45	  next : Option Row := none
    46	
    47	def Imgs.get (im : Imgs) : Img → Option Row
    48	  | .self => im.self
    49	  | .old => im.old
    50	  | .next => im.next
    51	
    52	def Term.eval (v : List Nat) (im : Imgs) : Term → Option Scalar
    53	  | .col i c => (im.get i).map (·.get c)
    54	  | .viewer => some (.id v)
    55	  | .param _ => none
    56	  | .lit x => some x
    57	
    58	/-- Evaluate every component of a key probe. -/
    59	def probeTerms (v : List Nat) (im : Imgs) (key : List (String × Term)) :
    60	    Option (List (String × Scalar)) :=
    61	  key.mapM fun p => Option.map (fun x => (p.1, x)) (p.2.eval v im)
    62	
    63	/-- Null returns no row only when it occurs in the selected unique point key.
    64	Nullable equalities outside that key are residual predicates and may match
    65	null. An empty `point` keeps the older all-components convention for the
    66	hand-written core model. -/
    67	def probeKey (v : List Nat) (im : Imgs) (point : List String)
    68	    (key : List (String × Term)) :
    69	    Option (List (String × Scalar)) :=
    70	  match probeTerms v im key with
    71	  | none => none
    72	  | some ks =>
    73	    let selected := if point.isEmpty then ks else ks.filter fun p => point.contains p.1
    74	    if selected.length == (if point.isEmpty then ks.length else point.eraseDups.length)
    75	        && !(selected.any fun p => p.2 == .null)
    76	    then some ks else none
    77	
    78	def keyMatch (ks : List (String × Scalar)) (r : Row) : Bool :=
    79	  ks.all fun p => decide (r.get p.1 = p.2)
    80	
    81	/-- Rule evaluation against the whole store `s`, for viewer `v`. -/
    82	def Rule.eval (s : Store) (v : List Nat) (im : Imgs) : Rule → Bool
    83	  | .allow => true
    84	  | .deny => false
    85	  | .eq a b => match a.eval v im, b.eval v im with
    86	    | some x, some y => decide (x = y)
    87	    | _, _ => false
    88	  | .ne a b => match a.eval v im, b.eval v im with
    89	    | some x, some y => decide (x ≠ y)
    90	    | _, _ => false
    91	  | .probe t point key => match probeKey v im point key with
    92	    | some ks => (s t).any (keyMatch ks)
    93	    | none => false
    94	  | .and a b => a.eval s v im && b.eval s v im
    95	  | .or a b => a.eval s v im || b.eval s v im
    96	  | .not a => !(a.eval s v im)
    97	
    98	/-- The tables a rule probes. -/
    99	def Rule.probes : Rule → List String
   100	  | .probe t _ _ => [t]
   101	  | .and a b | .or a b => a.probes ++ b.probes
   102	  | .not a => a.probes
   103	  | _ => []
   104	
   105	/-- The row columns (of the `self` image) a rule reads. -/
   106	def Term.selfCols : Term → List String
   107	  | .col .self c => [c]
   108	  | _ => []
   109	
   110	/-- Every term a rule compares or probes with, left to right. -/
   111	def Rule.terms : Rule → List Term
   112	  | .eq a b | .ne a b => [a, b]
   113	  | .probe _ _ key => key.map (·.2)
   114	  | .and a b | .or a b => a.terms ++ b.terms

   387	/// Every host prices a delete alike: the device's projection of the schema
   388	/// gives the reservation the full schema gives (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
   389	#[test]
   390	fn the_device_projection_prices_every_delete_as_the_server_does() {
   391	    for source in [PUBLIC.to_owned(), private(), PUBLIC.replace("table projects:\n  owner: principal", "table projects:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=1000 default ''\n  taskCount: count tasks by project")] {
   392	        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
   393	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
   394	        let projected = bound.schema.public_projection();
   395	        for name in bound.schema.tables.keys() {
   396	            assert_eq!(bound.schema.delete_reservation(name), projected.delete_reservation(name), "{name}");
   397	            assert_eq!(bound.schema.action_tables(name).written, projected.action_tables(name).written, "{name}'s event envelope");
   398	        }
   399	    }

   160	mutation admission): those proofs ignore it, as they ignore an index that is
   161	not unique. (A proof that the pinned value is non-null could recover the bound
   162	for one parent's group; §10 records it.) Cap enforcement, activation's group validation, and a device's
   163	cap proofs skip the null group of that column; changing the action away from
   164	`set null` re-validates the cap at activation, null group included.
   165	
   166	The unique inference has the same per-parent scope. It applies to required
   167	and optional references: a plain unique reference has at most one row for each
   168	non-null parent id. An optional unique reference may still have an unbounded
   169	null group. Referential actions probe only a concrete non-null parent id, so
   170	that null group is never counted in the action reservation and the inference
   171	never proves a table population bound. When both declarations exist, the
   172	effective bound is the smaller of the explicit cap and one.
   173	
   174	### 2.3 The reference index
   175	
   176	Every reference whose action probes or writes its children is probed by its
   177	column. The binder reuses a plain index whose leading columns are the
   178	reference (then, for an expiring child, its **effective** deadline column: a
   179	`read through` child's captured `$deadline`, which is the earlier of its own
   180	and its parents' deadlines, else its `expire at` column); otherwise it
   181	synthesizes an engine-named one: `[column, id]`, or
   182	`[column, deadlineColumn, id]` for an expiring child, so one seek skips
   183	an expired backlog (as `read through`'s child index does, LLP 3000.014 §6):
   184	the live rows are exactly `[id, null]` (never expires) and `[id]` with the
   185	deadline after now, so both seeks start at the live suffix and no expired row
   186	is ever visited.
   187	A `read through` reference uses its existing child index. The same index
   188	serves `cap N by <column>`. A `keep` reference gets none.
   189	
   190	## 3. What a delete does
   191	
   192	A delete of row `p` of table `P` — `delete P[id]`, `delete row`, a unique-key
   193	`delete P[key = …]`, a job, `POST /seed`'s delete, or `snapback4 data` —
   194	runs these steps in the deleting transaction, on every host:
   195	
   196	1. **Find and authorize `p`**, as today, with one addition: when any
   197	   `restrict`, `cascade` or `set null` reference names `P`, the caller needs
   198	   **read authority on `p`** as well as delete authority, for every delete
   199	   attempt, with or without children. A caller who may delete but not read
   200	   gets the ordinary hidden-target refusal. This is LLP 3000.014 §3.1's
   201	   rule, generalized from `read through` children to every referenced table,
   202	   and it keeps step 3 from probing a hidden row's children. The system
   203	   principal passes both rules, as it does every rule.
   204	2. **Reserve.** Charge the reservation `R(P)` of §5.1 on every meter, draw
   205	   its row count from the request's physical authorization allowance, and
   206	   publish the schema-fixed trace of §4.2.
   207	3. **Close.** Starting from `p`, follow every `cascade` reference to the
   208	   live rows that reference a reached row, and repeat: the **closure** `S`
   209	   is `p` plus every row reached. (`S` is finite: the cascade graph is
   210	   acyclic and each edge has an explicit or inferred effective bound.) Steps 3–5 stream the referencing rows
   211	   through the reference index (§2.3) a bounded page at a time and keep only
   212	   identities; no image outlives its page.
   213	4. **Restrict.** For every row `s` in `S` and every `restrict` reference
   214	   into `s`'s table, look for a live row that references `s` **and is not
   215	   itself in `S`**: the probe streams past the rows of `S` that name `s` —
   216	   `p` included, which may name itself — to the first live row outside `S`
   217	   (each row of `S` names one target per reference, so a delete's probes
   218	   visit each row of `S` at most once, plus one witness per probe). Every
   219	   such probe runs. If any is occupied, refuse with
   220	   the **first occupied reference in schema order** of
   221	   `(child table, column)` — never the first found while walking hidden
   222	   rows:
   223	
   224	   `E_REFERENCED: projects at <site> is still referenced through comments.task (on delete restrict); delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)`
   225	
   226	   "Live" means present and not expired. A row inserted earlier in the same
   227	   transaction counts; one deleted earlier in it does not. Because rows in
   228	   `S` are excluded, a diamond (`A` cascades to `B` and `C`, and `C`
   229	   restricts `B`) or a self-referencing child table deletes when the whole
   230	   closure goes together, as SQL's statement-end `NO ACTION` does. A `read
   231	   through` reference is not part of this pass: it keeps its own refusal,
   232	   `E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN`, and its stricter physical-child probe (LLP
   233	   3000.014 §3.3), evaluated as each row of `S` is removed in step 6. When
   234	   both would refuse, `E_REFERENCED` comes first (step 4 runs before any
   235	   removal).
   236	5. **Set null.** For every row `s` in `S` and every `set null` reference into
   237	   `s`'s table, clear the column of each live referencing row **not in
   238	   `S`** (deletion dominates: a row that is going away is not rewritten).
   239	   Each reference's group is gathered whole (ids only, at most its effective
   240	   bound: the explicit cap or inferred one)
   241	   before any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling whose
   242	   maintained column shares the reference's index and move it behind a page
   243	   cursor. The write rereads the row's current image immediately before clearing it
   244	   (an earlier clear's maintained write may have changed it), consults no
   245	   rule, and applies its constraints, maintains and partition events as any
   246	   write does.
   247	6. **Delete.** Remove the rows of `S` other than `p`, each table before every
   248	   table it must not outlive: its cascade parents and its `read through`
   249	   parent. Only those edges order the removal (an ordinary `restrict` or
   250	   `set null` reference between rows of `S` imposes none, since step 4
   251	   already cleared them), `check` refuses a cycle in their union
   252	   (`E_REFERENCE_ACTION`; e.g. `B` cascades from `P`, `C` cascades from `B`,
   253	   and `B` reads through `C`), and ties keep discovery order. Each row is
   254	   removed from its current image, consulting no rule and applying
   255	   constraints, maintains and partition events as any delete does. Then
   256	   remove `p` as today, **from its current image too**: the actions may have
   257	   changed it (a maintained column on `p` mirrors its children's removal), and
   258	   a store removes index entries from the image it is given. Authorization
   259	   stays the decision of step 1.
   260	
   261	Every step is inside the mutation's transaction: a refusal anywhere (a
   262	`restrict`, a read-through child, the meter) aborts the whole mutation, and
   263	nothing commits.
   264	
   265	**Expiry is a referential-action trigger.** A parent whose `expire at` passes
   266	is logically absent at that instant, independent of whether physical cleanup
   267	can retire it. Every path that physically retires an expired image — scheduled
   268	sweep, an already-expired birth at commit, and same-id replacement of an
   269	expired predecessor — applies steps 3–6 above in the same transaction. It
   270	bypasses only the expired root's authored read/delete authorization; it does
   271	not bypass `cascade`, `set null`, `restrict`, read-through retention,
   272	constraints, maintains or partition events on affected rows.
   273	
   274	A live `restrict` child, or a physically retained read-through child below a
   275	row in the cascade closure, keeps the expired root physically stored and the
   276	sweep continues to later due keys. The action walk preflights both kinds of
   277	blocker before its first set-null or cascade write. The retained root remains
   278	logically unreadable.
   279	Short sweeps preserve both a per-table row continuation and a rotating table
   280	continuation. Repeated bounded calls therefore cannot restart forever at the

  1341	fn validate_maintain(schema: &Schema, position: usize, maintain: &Maintain, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
  1342	    const GLOBAL: &str = "$global";
  1343	    let path = format!("maintains[{position}]");
  1344	    let issue = |issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>, message: String| issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.clone(), message });
  1345	    let Some(source) = schema.table(&maintain.source) else {
  1346	        issue(issues, format!("maintain names unknown source table {}", maintain.source));
  1347	        return;
  1348	    };
  1349	    let Some(target) = schema.table(&maintain.target) else {
  1350	        issue(issues, format!("maintain names unknown target table {}", maintain.target));
  1351	        return;
  1352	    };
  1353	    if let Some(key)=&maintain.row_key {
  1354	        if let Err(message)=key.validate(schema,maintain) {issue(issues,message);}
  1355	        return;
  1356	    }
  1357	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — stored value order cannot rank live sums between expiry and sweep; enforce at every schema entry point.
  1358	    if source.is_expiring() && target.indexes.values().any(|index| index.columns().iter().any(|column| column == "value")) {
  1359	        issue(issues, format!("maintain {} cannot order by value because source {} expires", maintain.target, maintain.source));
  1360	    }

  1397	    }

  1402	    }

  1404	        .chain(group_fields.iter().map(String::as_str))
  1405	        .chain(maintain.by.is_empty().then_some(GLOBAL))

  1420	    // readers who read none of the group's rows: aggregates sync one audience.
#[test]
fn w15c_old_value_indexes_and_expiry_refusal_are_unchanged() {
    for key in ["value, id", "author, value, id", "author, at, value, id"] {
        let b = compile(&format!("maintain totals: count posts by author, at\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n  by byCount: {key}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty() && b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert_eq!(b.schema.tables["totals"].indexes["byCount"].columns(), key.split(", ").collect::<Vec<_>>());
    }
    expiring_rank_rewrite_routes_by_window_intent();
    let source = SOURCE.replace("  at: time", "  until: time\n  by byUntil: until\n  expire at .until\n  at: time");
    for key in ["author, value, at, id", "author, value, at"] {
        let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("expiry.q".into(), format!("{source}maintain totals: count posts by author, at\n  read <- allow\n  by byCount: {key}\n"))]);
        assert_eq!(b.diagnostics[0].code, "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN_EXPIRE");
        assert_eq!(b.diagnostics[0].message, "maintain totals cannot order by value because source posts expires: stored order is stale before the physical sweep, so taking stored top-N can miss a live winner; a count upper-bound threshold scan is not built");
    }
}

#[test]
fn cards_pin_retirement_and_newest_first_column_recipe() {
    let maintains = snapback4_lang::guide::card("maintains").unwrap().split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    assert!(maintains.contains("A maintained view has rows `{ id, <group columns>, value }`."));
    let feeds = include_str!(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/legacy-feed-recipes.md"));
    let source = snapback4_lang::guide::quarry(feeds).into_iter().find(|s| s.contains("likeCount: count likes by postId")).unwrap();
    assert!(source.contains("by byHourLikes: hour(at), likeCount, at, id\n"));
    assert!(feeds.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ").contains("Equal scores put the newest post first"));
    let readme = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    assert!(readme.contains("`guide maintains`") && readme.contains("`guide feeds`"), "the orientation reaches both complete references");
    // The complete ordering law above and the recipe's key now live on these linked cards.
    let reference = format!("{maintains} {}", feeds.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" "));
    assert!(reference.contains("by byHourLikes: hour(at), likeCount, at, id") && reference.contains("Equal scores put the newest post first"));
    assert!(!reference.contains("ties use maintained id") && !readme.contains("ties use maintained id"));
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty() && b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn recorded_attribution_admission_and_nullable_keys() {
    let source = include_str!("fixtures/recorded-attribution.q");
    let compile = |source: &str| snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let good = compile(source);
    assert!(good.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", good.diagnostics);
    let likes = &good.schema.tables["likes"];
    assert_eq!(likes.recorded.len(), 3);
    assert!(likes.columns["postId.at"].is_optional());
    assert!(good.schema.tables["trend"].columns["hour"].is_optional());
    assert!(good.schema.operations["like"].sites.iter().any(|s| s.table == "posts" && s.kind == "recorded attribution"));
    let types = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&good);
    let likes_type = types.split("  likes: {").nth(1).unwrap().split("  };").next().unwrap();
    assert!(!likes_type.contains("postId."), "{likes_type}");
    for bad in [
        source.replacen("read <- allow", "read <- .authorId = viewer", 1),
        source.replace("  immutable postId\n", ""),
        source.replace("immutable authorId, parentId, at", "immutable parentId, at"),
        source.replace("by postId.authorId", "by postId.parentId.authorId"),
        source.replace("by postId.authorId", "by postId.unknown"),
        source.replace("where true by", "where postId.at > 0 by"),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&bad);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN" && d.guide == "maintains" && !d.rewrite.is_empty()), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
}

    16	#[test]
    17	fn global_count_is_zero_then_tracks_inserts_and_deletes() {
    18	    let source = r#"
    19	table entries:
    20	  public
    21	  insert <- allow
    22	  delete <- allow
    23	  online only
    24	table moderators:
    25	  principal: principal
    26	  unique principal
    27	  read <- .principal = viewer
    28	  online only
    29	view total: count entries
    30	  read <- allow
    31	  online only
    32	view privateTotal: count entries
    33	  read <- exists moderators[viewer]
    34	  online only
    35	mutation add(id: id):
    36	  return insert entries { id }
    37	mutation remove(id: entries):
    38	  delete entries[id]
    39	  return null
    40	query totalEntries():
    41	  return total
    42	query privateEntries():
    43	  return privateTotal
    44	"#;
    45	    let bound = checked(source);
    46	    let generated = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound);
    47	    let native = snapback4_lang::emit::rust::rust(&bound);
    48	    assert!(!generated.contains("$global") && !native.contains("$global"),
    49	        "the physical discriminator leaked into generated application types");
    50	    for expression in ["total first 1 by .id", "total[true]"] {
    51	        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery bad(c: cursor ?):\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
    52	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
    53	            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    54	    }
    55	    let mut engine = engine(source);
    56	    let query = |engine: &mut Engine| engine.query("totalEntries", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    57	    let private = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| engine.query("privateEntries", Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    58	    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
    59	    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Null, "an unauthorized empty global stays hidden");
    60	    engine.seed([("moderators".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
    61	        ("id", Value::Id("mod".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
    62	    ]))]).unwrap();
    63	    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(0), "authorized absence is the count identity");
    64	    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden absence is not zero");
    65	    for id in ["a", "b"] {
    66	        let outcome = engine.mutate("add", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id(id.into()))]))).unwrap();
    67	        assert!(matches!(outcome.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{outcome:?}");
    68	    }
    69	    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(2));
    70	    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(2));
    71	    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored scalar remains null");
    72	    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("a".into()))]))).unwrap();
    73	    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(1));
    74	    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("b".into()))]))).unwrap();
    75	    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
    76	    assert!(engine.store().rows("total").unwrap().is_empty(), "zero deletes the physical aggregate row");
    77	    assert!(engine.store().rows("privateTotal").unwrap().is_empty(), "private zero also has no physical row");
    78	}

    81	fn matching_count_guard_narrows_only_the_same_required_projection_until_a_write() {
    82	    let schema = r#"
    83	table entries:
    84	  owner: principal
    85	  score: int
    86	  optionalScore: int ?
    87	  by byOwner: owner, id
    88	  public
    89	  insert <- allow
    90	  delete <- allow
    91	  online only
    92	"#;
    93	    let sound = checked(&format!(r#"{schema}
    94	query stats(owner: principal):
    95	  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
    96	  require n > 0 else EMPTY
    97	  return {{ low: min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), high: max(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), total: sum(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) }}
    98	query filtered(owner: principal):
    99	  require count(entries where .owner = owner) > 0 else EMPTY
   100	  return min(entries where .owner = owner, .score) + 1
   101	mutation freshAfterWrite(owner: principal, victim: entries):
   102	  delete entries[victim]
   103	  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
   104	  require n > 0 else EMPTY
   105	  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
   106	"#));
   107	    let result = &sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "stats").unwrap().result;
   108	    assert!(matches!(result, Ty::Object(fields) if fields.values().all(|ty| ty == &Ty::Int)), "{result:?}");
   109	    assert_eq!(sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "filtered").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
   110	
   111	    for (name, body) in [
   112	        ("different", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries['other'] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   113	        ("optional", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .optionalScore) + 1"),
   114	        ("written", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  delete entries[victim]\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   115	        ("stale_binding", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  delete entries[victim]\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   116	        ("rebound", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   117	        ("joined", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  if flag:\n    n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   118	        ("root_rebound", "who = owner\n  n = count(entries[who] by byOwner)\n  who = next\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[who] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
   119	    ] {
   120	        let args = match name {
   121	            "written" | "stale_binding" => "owner: principal, victim: entries",
   122	            "joined" => "owner: principal, flag: bool",
   123	            "root_rebound" => "owner: principal, next: principal",
   124	            _ => "owner: principal",
   125	        };
   126	        let kind = if matches!(name, "written" | "stale_binding") { "mutation" } else { "query" };
   127	        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{schema}{kind} {name}({args}):\n  {body}\n"))]);
   128	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{name}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
   129	    }
   130	
   131	    let helper = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
   132	fn remove(victim: entries):
   133	  delete entries[victim]

   135	mutation staleHelper(owner: principal, victim: entries):
   136	  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)

     1	import Quarry.Write
     2	
     3	/-!
     4	# Running a program
     5	
     6	`run` takes (schema, helpers, store, viewer, program, arguments, ceiling)
     7	to an outcome. This is the function the later harness oracle calls.
     8	-/
     9	
    10	namespace Quarry
    11	
    12	/-- An outcome. A query answers with data, `complete` and the returned
    13	page's continuation (`next`, null when none), or is unavailable (a cut
    14	scalar read), or refuses. A mutation commits a result and a new store, or
    15	refuses (and changes nothing). -/
    16	inductive Outcome where
    17	  | answer (data : Value) (complete : Bool) (next : Value)
    18	  | unavailable
    19	  | committed (result : Value) (store : Store)
    20	  | refused (code : String)
    21	
    22	/-- The world of a query: the view; every write refuses. -/
    23	def queryHooks : Hooks View where
    24	  view _ vw := vw
    25	  apply _ _ _ := throw (.refuse "E_QUARRY_PLACEMENT")
    26	
    27	/-- The world of a mutation: the store; reads see its visible rows. -/
    28	def storeHooks : Hooks Store where
    29	  view cx s := visibleRowsAt cx.schema s cx.viewer cx.now
    30	  apply := applyWrite
    31	
    32	def bindArgs (params : List Param) (args : List (String × Value)) : Except Stop Env :=
    33	  params.mapM fun p => match args.lookup p.name with
    34	    | some v => pure (p.name, (v, none))
    35	    | none => if p.optional then pure (p.name, (.null, none)) else throw (.refuse "E_INPUT")
    36	
    37	def defaultFuel : Nat := 100000
    38	
    39	structure Case where
    40	  schema : Schema
    41	  helpers : List Helper := []
    42	  store : Store
    43	  viewer : List Nat
    44	  now : Int := 0
    45	  program : Program
    46	  args : List (String × Value) := []
    47	  budget : Option Nat := none
    48	  /-- Run only the server expiry sweep and return its resulting store. -/
    49	  sweepOnly : Bool := false
    50	
    51	def Case.ctx (c : Case) : Ctx :=
    52	  { schema := c.schema, viewer := c.viewer, now := c.now, helpers := c.helpers, budget := c.budget,
    53	    mutation := c.program.kind == .mutation }
    54	
    55	def flowValue : Flow → Value × Value
    56	  | .normal => (.null, .null)
    57	  | .ret v nx => (v, nx.getD .null)
    58	
    59	def runQuery (c : Case) : Outcome :=
    60	  match bindArgs c.program.params c.args with
    61	  | .error (.refuse code) => .refused code
    62	  | .error .unavailable => .unavailable
    63	  | .ok env =>
    64	    let vw := visibleRowsAt c.schema c.store c.viewer c.now
    65	    match (execStmts queryHooks defaultFuel c.ctx vw env c.program.body).run {} with
    66	    | .ok ((fl, _, _), rs) => let (v, nx) := flowValue fl; .answer v rs.complete nx
    67	    | .error (.refuse code) => .refused code
    68	    | .error .unavailable => .unavailable
    69	
    70	def runMutation (c : Case) : Outcome :=
    71	  match bindArgs c.program.params c.args with
    72	  | .error (.refuse code) => .refused code
    73	  | .error .unavailable => .unavailable
    74	  | .ok env =>
    75	    match (execStmts storeHooks defaultFuel c.ctx c.store env c.program.body).run {} with
    76	    | .ok ((fl, s', _), _) => .committed (flowValue fl).1 s'
    77	    | .error (.refuse code) => .refused code
    78	    | .error .unavailable => .refused "E_BOUND"
    79	
    80	def run (c : Case) : Outcome :=
    81	  if c.sweepOnly then .committed .null (sweepExpired c.schema c.store c.now)
    82	  else match c.program.kind with
    83	    | .query => runQuery c
    84	    | .mutation => runMutation c
    85	
    86	end Quarry

+  public

   295	
   296	/// `lean--{field}--{engine class}~{model class}`.
   297	pub fn signature(field: &str, e: &Canon, m: &Canon) -> String {
   298	    format!("lean--{}--{}~{}", field.replace('_', "-"), e.class(field), m.class(field))
   299	}
   300	
   301	fn finding(field: &str, e: &Canon, m: &Canon, engine: Outcome, model: &Json, trail: Vec<Outcome>) -> Verdict {
   302	    Verdict::Disagree(Finding {
   303	        signature: signature(field, e, m),
   304	        oracle: Oracle::Lean,
   305	        field: field.into(),
   306	        left: engine,
   307	        right: Outcome { kind: "lean".into(), value: model.clone() },
   308	        continuation: trail,
   309	        store_trace: None,
   310	    })
   311	}
   312	
   313	fn trail_entry(e: &Canon, m: &Canon) -> Outcome {
   314	    Outcome { kind: "canonical".into(), value: json!({"engine": e.json(), "model": m.json()}) }
   315	}
   316	
   317	/// What one case comes to; `executed` counts engine and model calls.
   318	pub struct Checked {
   319	    pub verdict: Verdict,
   320	    pub executed: u64,
   321	    pub pages: u64,
   322	    pub mutation: bool,
   323	    /// The first outcome's class on an agreeing case (`committed`, a code,
   324	    /// `unavailable`, or the data's shape).
   325	    pub class: String,
   326	}
   327	
   328	/// The module, or why the case is outside the fragment.
   329	pub fn module(source: &str) -> Result<LeanModule, String> {
   330	    let (document, diagnostics) = snapback4_lang::parse_document("schema.q", source);
   331	    if !diagnostics.is_empty() {
   332	        return Err("parse".into());
   333	    }
   334	    snapback4_lang::emit::lean::from_documents(&[document]).map_err(|u| u.reason)
   335	}
   336	
   337	/// Compare one case whose source compiles. `Err` is a harness or emitter
   338	/// defect (never a verdict).
   339	pub fn compare(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Checked, String> {
   340	    let unsupported = |reason: String| Ok(Checked { verdict: Verdict::Unsupported(reason), executed: 0, pages: 0, mutation: false, class: String::new() });
   341	    let module = match module(source) {
   342	        Ok(m) => m,
   343	        Err(reason) => return unsupported(reason),
   344	    };
   345	    let mutation = case.program.mutation();
   346	    if let Err(u) = module.program("run") {
   347	        return unsupported(u.reason);
   348	    }
   349	    if mutation {
   350	        if let Err(u) = module.program("observe") {
   351	            return unsupported(format!("observe-{}", u.reason));
   352	        }
   353	    }
   354	    let store = match module.store(&case.rows) {
   355	        Ok(s) => s,
   356	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   357	    };
   358	    let mut m_args = match module.tag_args("run", &case.args) {
   359	        Ok(a) => a,
   360	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   361	    };
   362	    // The prelude: each step on the engine and on the model's store, in order.
   363	    for i in 0..case.steps.len() {
   364	        if let Err(u) = module.program(&format!("prep{i}")) {
   365	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   366	        }
   367	        if let Err(u) = module.tag_args(&format!("prep{i}"), &case.steps[i].args) {
   368	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   369	        }
   370	    }

codex
I found a material issue in the new preflight: it treats every physical read-through child as a blocker, including children already scheduled for deletion in the cascade closure. I’m checking the admitted schema shapes and deletion order to pin down the affected case.

   429	/// Why rows of `name` cannot be deleted or cleared by another row's delete.
   430	pub fn write_target_refusal(schema: &Schema, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
   431	    let table = schema.table(name)?;
   432	    if table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::AppendOnly)) { Some(format!("{name} is append only")) }
   433	    else if !table.written_by.is_empty() { Some(format!("{name} has field-owned (`written by`) columns, whose owners the schema singled out")) }
   434	    else if schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| m.source == name || m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| k.table == name))
   435	        .flat_map(|m| [Some(m.target.as_str()), m.private.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.table.as_str())]).flatten()
   436	        .any(|destination| schema.table(destination).is_some_and(|t| t.sync.is_some() && t.written_by.values().any(|authority| !authority.audience))) {
   437	        Some(format!("{name}'s derived writes deliver private fields, whose recipient authorization has no fixed action price"))
   438	    }
   439	    else if schema.timeline_member(name) { Some(format!("a timeline orders {name}'s writes")) }
   440	    else if schema.maintains.iter().any(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| k.table == name)) { Some(format!("{name} is a row-keyed view's member table, whose writes may recount a group outside any fixed price")) }
   441	    else if schema.maintains.iter().any(|m| m.source == name && matches!(m.kind, MaintainKind::Sum { .. })) { Some(format!("{name} feeds a maintained sum: system work can delete rows despite `delete <- deny`, and a cascade from another parent can reach them; a hidden contribution could push the sum out of range, so whether the delete commits would depend on rows the deleter cannot see")) }
   442	    else if schema.write_reservation(name).is_none_or(|plan| plan.server_only) { Some(format!("{name}'s writes have no fixed price, so a cascade into it cannot be reserved")) }
   443	    else { None }
   444	}

    78	fn groups() -> Engine {
